# Beast Families B01-B03 — Crest-cocks (2) + Carrion crows (2) + Highland raptors (2), Phase 1, no JSON
Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Beast. Crest-cocks animal vs Fiend corpse. Crows battlefield, no society. Raptors high plateau chain, non-sentient vs folk.

## 51. ENY_CREST_COCK — Red Crest-Cock
Strutting red cocks rule farmyard middens where grain spills daily beneath threshing floors and carts. They crow challenges at dawn tirelessly, guarding hens with spurred feet and slashing beaks. After fox bounty lapsed during grain wars, their free-ranging flocks spread across green valley farms. Farm wives mark killers with red thread anklets, culling rogues that savage chicks without warning. Ordinary villagers prize loud guardians for snake warnings while cursing ruined seedlings yearly. In battle they launch at faces suddenly, raking eyes with spurs and beating wings violently. Thick gloves and lowered buckets fend attacks effectively during feeding in occupied yards. Stoats and hard winters thin flocks locally, though sheltered coops preserve breeders through frost.
Combat: 2/1/3/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Compact red bird stands knee-high proudly, comb scarlet and wattles swinging boldly. Tail sickles iridescent green-black, spurs hooked like thorns, eyes amber and fearless. States: midden-strutting crowing guard / launching face-raking spur assault / glove-fended fluttering stagger / neck-wrung limp defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via comb, reason: fowl sexes differ clearly): scrawny chick fluff / red rooster male / brown hen female / capon quiet keeper.

## 52. ENY_BLACK_CRESTCOCK — Black Moor Cock
Glossy black moor cocks haunt peat bog edges where midges swarm thickest at dusk hours. They dance in circles stubbornly, stamping feet to flush worms from saturated mossy ground. After peat fires cleared nesting cover downhill, their displaced leks crowded highland pasture fringes badly. Shepherds stake flash strips around folds nightly, diverting dancers away from lambing ewes for safety. Ordinary bog folk eat surplus eggs gladly while respecting dancers as weather omens traditionally. In battle they swarm boots collectively, pecking laces with bruising rapid jabs repeatedly. Stamped earth and raised cots prevent most harassment effectively during nights in occupied bogs. Harriers and flooded leks thin numbers yearly, though high moss preserves breeders through winters.
Combat: 3/1/2/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Slender black dancer gleams green-purple in sunlight, crest upright like a crown. Legs orange starkly, dance stamps rhythmically, call booming hollow across flat bogs. States: lek-circling stamping dance / swarming boot-pecking harassment assault / strip-diverted milling stagger / harrier-seized limp defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via lek role, reason: moor cock sexes differ in display): mottled chick runner / black lekking male / brown nesting female / peat-stained silent keeper.

## 53. ENY_CARRION_CROW — Black Carrion Crow
Wary black crows follow marching columns where field kitchens discard scraps along dusty roadsides. They memorize patrol schedules exactly, arriving moments after sentries abandon checkpoint cook fires. After successor battles littered green valley fields, their wheeling flocks became dreaded defeat omens. Sextons stretch gut strings over fresh graves daily, tangling wings of early scavengers effectively. Ordinary soldiers curse their croaking presence while valuing warnings of approaching cavalry dust. In battle they dive at wounds collectively, pecking bandages with persistent filthy beaks repeatedly. Smoked bandages and covered litters prevent attention reliably during retreats through occupied fields. Hawks and poisoned baits thin flocks locally, though rookeries shelter breeders high in cliffs.
Combat: 2/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: Medium black bird glides warily, beak stout and wings broad-fingered for soaring. Eyes grey intelligent, call harsh and mocking, flock wheels tightly before every descent. States: column-following waiting glide / diving wound-pecking harassment assault / hawk-scattered croaking stagger / bait-poisoned fallen defeat. Variants (2M/2F subtle, flock-forms, reason: crow sexes differ only by size): grey fledgling hopper / black column follower / broad-winged rook male-presenting / narrow-billed female-presenting.

## 54. ENY_GREY_HOODED_CROW — Grey Hooded Crow
Grey-hooded crows patrol wetland causeways where eels strand after every receding autumn flood. They pry mussels with dropped stones cleverly, teaching juveniles tool tricks along paved banks. After toll bridges priced crossings beyond reach, their jeering guards mocked dripping travelers daily. Travelers toss crusts downhill deliberately, bribing escorts away from packs and purses for safety. Ordinary ferrymen share catches willingly, valuing storm warnings screamed before sudden squalls arrive. In battle they mob heads relentlessly, buffeting hats with wings and snatching at eyes. Wide hats and raised staves deter mobs effectively during crossings through occupied causeways. Otters and hard freezes thin numbers locally, though willow rookeries preserve breeders through winters.
Combat: 2/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: Smart grey-and-black crow appears hooded formally, body ash-grey contrasting black wings. Tool use visible with stone caches, juveniles tutored patiently, voice varied mimic-rich. States: causeway-patrolling jeering glide / mobbing head-buffeting snatch assault / staff-deterred scattering stagger / otter-pounced fallen defeat. Variants (sexes subtle, hood-forms, reason: hooded sexes differ only by hood shade): ash fledgling beggar / grey causeway mocker / dark-hooded male-presenting / pale-hooded female-presenting.

## 55. ENY_HIGHLAND_RAPTOR — Brown Highland Raptor
Broad brown raptors ride high plateau thermals where marmots whistle warnings across scree slopes. They stoop half a league screaming faintly, striking with talons extended like grappling hooks. After bounty pelts priced foxes beyond trapping, their unmolested eyries multiplied along cliff rims. Cliff herders tether kids with short ropes hourly, preventing snatches during lambing season on heights. Ordinary highland folk respect sky hunters as weather prophets while guarding poultry fiercely always. In battle they stoop from sun glare suddenly, raking helms with armor-piercing talon slashes. Locked shields and thorny hedges prevent stoops effectively during marches across open plateaus. Eagle owls and rockfalls thin eyries yearly, though sheer cliffs shelter breeders through storms.
Combat: 4/1/3/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Broad brown hunter spans a cart width fully, wings fingered deeply for thermal soaring. Talons black and massive, beak hooked cruelly, eyes gold and unblinking. States: thermal-riding circling vigil / sun-glare stooping talon assault / hedge-foiled climbing stagger / owl-pierced grounded defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via size, reason: raptor females outweigh males clearly): downy eyas screamer / brown thermal rider / smaller tiercel male / massive brooding female.

## 56. ENY_CLIFF_RAPTOR — Grey Cliff Raptor
Slate-grey cliff raptors nest inside sea cliffs where salt spray blinds unwary climbers every morning. They shear past ledges within handspans daringly, snatching gulls that stray from flock formations. After lighthouse keepers were withdrawn during budget cuts, their undisturbed stacks became hazards to pigeons. Pigeon fanciers fly flocks at noon only now, avoiding dawn patrols that reap stragglers mercilessly. Ordinary cliff folk gather molted feathers for fletching while cursing stolen chickens yearly. In battle they slash from fog suddenly, opening cheeks with razor wingtip slashes before vanishing. Netted caps and fog horns deter slashes effectively during work on exposed cliff paths. Ravens and crumbling ledges thin nests yearly, though sea stacks preserve breeders through gales.
Combat: 5/1/2/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Streamlined grey dart appears falcon-like but bulkier, wings scythe-narrow for cliff shearing. Breast barred finely, tail rudders precisely, stoop whistles audibly through foggy air. States: ledge-shearing patrol glide / fog-slashing cheek-cut assault / horn-deterred veering stagger / raven-mobbed grounded defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via bulk, reason: cliff sexes differ in bulk): barred fledgling skimmer / slate ledge shearer male / broad-chested nesting female / salt-bleached stack keeper.

# Beast Families B04-B06 — Forest owls (2) + Wetland herons (2) + Delta gulls (2), Phase 1, no JSON
Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Beast. Owls forested lake chain. Herons wetland chain. Gulls river delta chain. All non-sentient vs folk.

## 57. ENY_FOREST_OWL — Brown Forest Owl
Silent brown owls haunt forested lakeshores where voles rustle beneath rotting leaf litter nightly. They glide without whisper between mossy trunks patiently, striking before prey hears any wingbeat. After charcoal burners thinned underbrush for kilns, their hunting lanes widened along new rides. Riders lash lanterns low on saddles nightly, avoiding low stoops that snatch caps from heads. Ordinary timber folk welcome vole control warmly while fearing omens hooted before each death. In battle they swoop at lanterns suddenly, clawing faces with silent extensions of talons. Hooded lamps and broad hats prevent injuries effectively during night work in occupied woods. Martens and hard frosts thin nests yearly, though hollow oaks shelter breeders through winters.
Combat: 3/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Broad brown owl looms cat-sized, facial disks pale and heart-shaped around black eyes. Wings fringed silently, talons needle-sharp, hoot deep and carrying across still water. States: trunk-gliding silent patrol / swooping face-clawing lantern assault / lamp-hooded circling stagger / marten-raided fallen defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via bulk, reason: owl females outweigh males): fluffy owlet squeaker / brown lakeshore hunter / smaller tiercel male / massive brooding female.

## 58. ENY_DEEPWOOD_OWL — Grey Deepwood Owl
Huge grey owls rule deep forest interiors where ancient yews block moonlight for weeks. They mimic dripping water patiently, luring thirsty rodents toward waiting talons in darkness. After deep trails were abandoned during border wars, their undisturbed territories expanded across whole watersheds. Charcoal runners mark nesting yews with white rags carefully, steering night porters around brooding giants. Ordinary deep travelers dread soft chuckling calls more than wolves, for disorientation follows quickly. In battle they envelop heads with broad wings suddenly, driving victims into roots with buffets. Linked arms and low crouches prevent separations effectively during passages through occupied yews. Goshawks and windthrows thin nests rarely, though cavernous hollows shelter breeders through storms.
Combat: 4/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: Massive ash-grey owl towers child-high when perched, wings spanning a full cart width. Call chuckling and humanlike unsettlingly, talons wrist-thick, eyes glowing amber in torchlight. States: yew-perched mimicking vigil / enveloping head-buffeting drive assault / crouch-linked foiled stagger / windthrown grounded defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via bulk, reason: deepwood females dominate clearly): grey owlet hisser / yew interior elder male / deep-hooted displaying male / cavernous brooding female.

## 59. ENY_WETLAND_HERON — Grey Wetland Heron
Patient grey herons spear shallows where frogs chorus loudest after warm spring rains arrive. They stand like weathered stakes for hours immobile, stabbing faster than watching eyes follow. After eel weirs blocked spawning runs upstream, their spearing perches concentrated around sluice outflows badly. Sluice keepers drape nets over channels nightly, sparing fry from relentless dagger beaks for market. Ordinary reed-cutters respect spear-fishers as eel rivals while gathering molted plumes for hats. In battle they jab at eyes deliberately, driving intruders from nests with pickaxe beak thrusts. Raised shields and wide detours prevent injuries effectively during nesting season in occupied marshes. Otters and drained fens thin colonies locally, though flooded willows preserve rookeries through summers.
Combat: 3/1/3/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Tall grey fisher stands waist-high elegantly, neck coiled like a spring before strikes. Beak dagger-long and yellow, legs black and stilt-thin, crest plumes trailing in breeding season. States: stake-still shallows vigil / jabbing eye-targeting beak assault / shield-deterred retreating stagger / otter-raided fallen defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via plume, reason: heron sexes differ in crest length): streaked juvenile stalker / grey sluice spearfisher / long-crested displaying male / short-crested nesting female.

## 60. ENY_WHITE_HERON — White Fen Heron
Graceful white herons adorn festival marshes where tourists toss minnows from punts each summer afternoon. They preen snow-bright plumage obsessively between bouts, staining bills pink with shrimp feasts daily. After plume hunters were outlawed by marsh councils wisely, their rookeries rebounded across protected fens. Councils license viewing punts strictly each dawn, funding wardens with tourist coppers for patrols. Ordinary punters adore photo poses while wardens warn against feeding bread that sickens chicks. In battle they slash with wing claws unexpectedly, raking arms that reach toward nests too closely. Pole-pushed distance and covered baskets prevent clashes effectively during passages through rookeries. Foxes and late frosts thin chicks yearly, though rafts of reeds shelter nests through floods.
Combat: 2/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Slender snow-white wader glows against green reeds strikingly, neck sinuous and impossibly long. Plumes lacy in breeding dress, bill orange-pink seasonally, legs black contrasting brilliantly. States: punt-side preening display / slashing wing-claw nest assault / pole-distanced retreating stagger / fox-raided fallen defeat. Variants (2M/2F readable via plumes, reason: white heron sexes differ in display): fluffy white chick / snow fen preener / lacy-plumed displaying male / neat-plumed nesting female.

## 61. ENY_DELTA_GULL — Grey Delta Gull
Bold grey gulls mob ferry wakes where discarded scraps trail behind every laden river barge. They scream insults constantly overhead shrilly, snatching bread from children's hands with precision. After fish-gut dumping was banned in harbors wisely, their screaming clouds followed barges farther upstream. Barge cooks toss peelings astern on schedule daily, bribing escorts away from drying fish racks. Ordinary deckhands curse droppings on ropes while valuing storm warnings screamed before squalls. In battle they mob faces collectively, blinding lookouts with wings and pecking at eyes. Oiled canvas covers and swung mops deter mobs effectively during passages through occupied wakes. Skuas and oiled spills thin flocks locally, though dune rookeries preserve breeders through winters.
Combat: 2/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: Stocky grey-and-white gull rides wakes buoyantly, wings broad and wingtips darkly banded. Beak hooked for snatching, eyes ringed redly, cry raucous and instantly recognizable everywhere. States: wake-riding screaming escort / mobbing face-blinding peck assault / mop-deterred scattering stagger / oiled grounded defeat. Variants (2M/2F subtle, wake-forms, reason: gull sexes differ only by bill spot): speckled fledgling beggar / grey wake rider / red-spotted displaying male / dull-billed nesting female.

## 62. ENY_STORM_GULL — White Storm Gull
Hardy white gulls ride coastal storms where waves smash harbor walls with thunderous green force. They lean into gales joyfully, skimming troughs that swamp smaller birds within moments. After beacon fires were shuttered during harbor disputes, their storm-leading flights guided smugglers through reefs. Smugglers follow white wings through fog gratefully, trusting birds to mark safe channels by instinct. Ordinary sailors trust storm wings as living barometers while lashing cargo against coming seas. In battle they ram with keeled breasts forcefully, knocking caps loose and breaking lantern glass. Battened hatches and lowered sails prevent damage effectively during passages through occupied storms. Peregrines and starvation thin flocks in calms, though cliff ledges shelter breeders through winters.
Combat: 3/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: Pure white storm rider gleams against grey seas vividly, larger and heavier than delta cousins. Breast keeled like a prow, wings stiff against gales, cry deeper and wilder. States: gale-leaning storm riding / ramming breast-battering charge assault / battened foiled stagger / calm-starved grounded defeat. Variants (2M/2F subtle, storm-forms, reason: storm gull sexes differ only by wing bar): grey fledgling roller / white storm rider / dark-barred displaying male / plain-winged nesting female.

## 63. ENY_CLIFF_SWIFT — Grey Cliff Swift
Frenetic grey swifts stitch cliff faces where midges rise thickly after summer rains end. They sleep on the wing impossibly, circling for weeks without landing on stone or earth. After signal mirrors were mounted along highland forts wisely, their shattered flocks learned new lanes. Signalmen shutter mirrors at dusk hourly, preventing collisions that rain stunned birds onto parapets. Ordinary cliff children time races by swift passes while learning wind reading from banking flocks. In battle they slash across eyes blindingly, drawing blood with sickle wingtips at speed. Goggled leather caps prevent injuries effectively during nesting season on occupied ledges always. Hobbies and hailstorms thin flocks yearly, though deep crevices shelter breeders through winters.
Combat: 2/1/5/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Tiny grey dart appears all wings and gape, body torpedo-small beneath scythe wings. Tail forks narrowly, eyes bead darkly, speed blurs shapes beyond tracking easily. States: face-stitching circling patrol / slashing eye-drawing speed assault / cap-foiled veering stagger / hail-grounded limp defeat. Variants (sexes subtle, swift-forms, reason: swift sexes differ only by throat patch): sooty fledgling screamer / grey cliff stitcher / white-throated displaying male / dusky-throated nesting female.

## 64. ENY_CINDER_SWIFT — Black Cinder Swift
Soot-black swifts haunt volcanic updrafts where ash midges swarm above cooling lava fields. They drink by skimming crater lakes daringly, trailing bellies through sulfurous spray without harm. After obsidian miners lit warming braziers along rims nightly, their singed scouts learned to detour widely. Miners douse braziers during fledging weeks willingly, sparing young wings from fatal scorching drafts. Ordinary ash folk read low circles as eruption warnings while covering cisterns against falling grit. In battle they buffet torches collectively, fanning flames toward hands with beating wings dangerously. Doused torches and crouched postures prevent burns effectively during crossings through occupied updrafts. Peregrines and sulfur chokes thin flocks locally, though lava tubes shelter breeders through eruptions.
Combat: 3/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: Compact black ember darts faster than grey cousins visibly, wings edged rusty like cooling iron. Throat flecked ash-white, tail shorter and stouter, cry shriller and more urgent. States: updraft-riding ash patrol / buffeting torch-fanning flame assault / doused retreating stagger / choke-grounded limp defeat. Variants (sexes subtle, cinder-forms, reason: cinder swift sexes differ only by edge color): ash-flecked fledgling / black updraft rider / rust-edged displaying male / soot-edged nesting female.

## 65. ENY_PEACOCK_RAPTOR — Green Peacock Raptor
Vain green hunters fan jeweled tails across green valley leks where hens judge every quivering eyespot. They rattle quills before charges deliberately, hypnotizing rabbits that freeze before talon strikes. After vineyard walls offered new perches uphill, their displaying lines stretched across terraced rows boldly. Vintners hang mirrored shards among vines cunningly, diverting dancers away from ripening grapes for harvest. Ordinary valley children imitate rattling dances endlessly while gathering molted plumes for festival masks. In battle they flare tails massively, dazzling foes before raking shins with spurred hind legs. Averted eyes and raised shields prevent dazzle effectively during passages through occupied leks. Foxes and snares thin dancers yearly, though thorny thickets shelter breeders through hunting seasons.
Combat: 4/1/3/2, always_physical.

## 1. ENY_GIANT_SPIDER — Giant Web-Spinner (reuse)
Grey web-spinners string cart-rope webs across abandoned barns where thatch has rotted through. They hang motionless for days above livestock pens, feeling every footstep through trembling silk lines. After the fracture emptied toll roads, their webs spread unchecked across granaries and empty watchtowers. Timber-town trappers harvest their silk for bowstrings, paying scarred collectors by the measured skein. Market-town mothers warn children from haylofts where spinners nest thickest in late summer. In battle they drop silently onto shoulders, pinning shields with sticky strands before biting exposed necks. Burning torches drive them backward, for singed spinnerets cannot spin and panic spreads quickly. Villagers burn infested barns each autumn, then rebuild with tarred beams that discourage new anchoring.
Combat: reuse_enemy_id ENY_GIANT_SPIDER.
Appearance: Barrel-bodied grey spinner looms large as a calf, eight jointed legs spanning a cart width. Coarse bristles mat its back, spinnerets pulse pale, and twin fangs drip amber venom. States: web-hung motionless vigil / dropping silk-pinning bite assault / torch-singed curling stagger / leg-folded collapsed defeat. Variants (sexes indistinguishable, age-forms only, reason: spider sexes too subtle for battlefield readability): pale barn juvenile / tar-dark granary elder / scarred silk-hook male-presenting / mottled harvester female-presenting.

## 2. ENY_WEB_RECLUSE — Grey Recluse Spinner
Solitary recluse spinners line dry cellars with low trip-threads that quiver before every strike. They shun sunlit barns entirely, preferring stone foundations where damp keeps their joints supple. After successor tolls thinned rat-catchers, their numbers thickened beneath market-town warehouses and granaries. Warehouse porters test corners with brooms each morning, listening for the faint papery rustle of legs. Ordinary folk dread their shy bite more than wolves, for swelling lingers many painful days afterward. In battle they dart from cracks, nipping ankles swiftly before retreating into shadowed crevices again. Crushed garlic and strong vinegar drive them outward, a trick every cellar-keeper learns early. Burned thatch and lime-washed walls discourage nesting, though vigilant sweeping remains the surest protection.
Combat: 4/1/2/1, always_physical.

Variants (sexes indistinguishable, age-forms only, reason: recluse sexes invisible at combat distance): cellar pale juvenile / warehouse ash elder / violin-dark male-presenting / purse-bellied female-presenting.

## 3. ENY_GIANT_SCORPION — Giant Sand Stinger
Broad sand stingers patrol sun-cracked flats where sparse scrub offers almost no sheltering shade. They dig shallow scrapes beneath thorn bushes, waiting motionless until beetles blunder within reach. After imperial caravans ceased watering southern wells, their burrows multiplied around every neglected trough. Highland-fort outriders prod suspicious sand with lances before allowing thirsty mules to drink deeply. Ordinary drovers fear their tail more than bandits, for even weak venom fells a working mule. In battle they sidle sideways constantly, snapping pincers at shields before arching tails over guards. Heavy boots and thick leggings turn most stings aside, a lesson every caravan guard learns quickly. Villagers flood burrows with brine each spring, then collapse tunnels with ox-drawn stone rollers.

Variants (sexes subtle, size-morphs only, reason: scorpion sexes unreadable in battle): pale sand juvenile / amber flat elder / broad-pincer male-presenting / deep-bellied female-presenting.

## 4. ENY_BURROW_SCORPION — Dune Burrow Scorpion
Deep burrow scorpions engineer branching tunnels beneath dune troughs where trapped moisture lingers longest. They plug entrances with sand pellets each dawn, hiding airflow from keen jackals and hungry birds. After wetland drainage drove mole-catchers northward, their mounds spread across fallow millet fields unchecked. ## 5. ENY_MILLIPEDE_TUNNELLER — Black Millipede Tunneller

## 6. ENY_GARDEN_MILLIPEDE — Striped Garden Millipede
Striped garden millipedes patrol compost heaps behind market-town houses where vegetable peelings rot deeply. They march nightly along mortar joints, cleaning mould that would otherwise sicken stored grain supplies. After siege gardens replaced flower courts, their quiet service earned tolerance from even fastidious cooks. City wives sweep them gently into jars, releasing workers back onto garden beds after inspections. Pale house centipedes haunt high ceiling corners where warm air carries flies upward every evening. Sideways river crabs crowd delta shallows where reeds trap drifting food after every high tide. They brandish mismatched claws boldly, backing into rents between slippery stones when threatened suddenly. Aggressive snapping crabs guard tidal pools where children wade barefoot during warm summer afternoons. They bury flat in sand instantly, leaving only watchful eyestalks above the rippled surface. After storm tides breached oyster beds, their broken-shell middens marked every high-water line clearly. Oyster divers wear stout mittens now, prying shells quickly before guards return to patrol. ## 1. ENY_LUCIFER_SATAN — Lucifer-Satan, First of the Fallen
The first Fallen was once the brightest singer of dawn-praise before any throne existed. After pride turned his perfection inward, he now keeps court in ash-choked highland calderas. Successor warlords send envoys bearing tribute uphill yearly, begging blessings that always cost souls. Market-town preachers name every failed harvest after him while selling lead charms against pride always. Ordinary folk leave one chair empty at weddings fearfully lest the uninvited guest arrive. In battle he answers challenges with courtesy coldly, unmaking shields with words before swords move. No wall bars his entry anywhere, for every lock remembers being forged in pride. Whether Lucifer and Satan are one being or two remains an open question for sages.

His voice carries choirs that kneel listeners unasked, and his shadow falls upward like smoke. States: caldera-court enthroned address / courteous unmaking word-blade assault / unanswered name faltering stagger / dawn-returned banished defeat. No variants apply: this Demon's unique identity replaces flavour variants.

## 2. ENY_SAMAEL — Samael the Accuser
The second Fallen was once severity itself, the uncompromising seriousness guarding Creation against corruption. After severity lost mercy, he now hunts guilt through every soul without rest or pity. Desert hermits carve his hundred names on cliff walls hopefully, begging exemption from accusation. River-port judges keep a black second seal fearfully for verdicts they dare not sign. Ordinary mourners whisper his name at gravesides unwillingly when grief curdles into blame. In battle he reads every sin aloud from memory, stunning lines before striking with venom. No confession satisfies his ledgers ever, for each absolution reveals three deeper faults. Sages debate whether his venom is punishment or envy wearing judgment robes today.
Combat: 9/8/8/7, cycle (accusation-venom flagged proposal; never final).
Appearance: He presents masculine and mature, gaunt with prosecutorial severity. A gaunt accuser robed in prosecutorial grey, quills and scrolls rustling without wind around him. His eyes are inkwells that never empty, and his pointing finger leaves bruises like seals. The fifth Fallen once acquired and preserved knowledge for the proper guidance of Creation. After wisdom lost restraint, no sacrifice or boundary is unacceptable in his pursuit of knowing. The sixth Fallen once preserved Creation's knowledge, measure, and memory against all decay. After record lost humility, what is written becomes more authoritative than reality itself. The seventh Fallen once allowed truth to be discovered, expressed, and taught freely. After understanding lost openness, knowledge must be protected from error by suppressing unacceptable ideas. Riverbank teachers test currents with flower boats yearly before lessons, begging clarity that does not condemn. The eighth Fallen once applied divine wisdom to practical problems with mercy intact. After reason lost compassion, people become variables and sacrifices become acceptable solutions. Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Fiend, unnatural hostile entities. Origins/societies/magic TBD flagged, never final.

## 1. ENY_STONE_SENTINEL — Mossy Stone Sentinel (Golem)
Mossy sentinels squat at collapsed bridgeheads where imperial milestones sink into black marsh water. No mason claims their jointing patiently, for mortar seams fit tighter than any living chisel achieves. After flood years drowned toll records uphill unfortunately, their silent vigils outlasted every garrison posted nearby. Ordinary villagers hang straw charms on fingers fearfully while whitewashing warnings for children at play. In battle they rise without sound suddenly, crushing shields with millstone fists that shatter oak. Burning pitch poured into joints slows giants effectively, buying moments to flee across planks. Sappers undermine plinths with iron bars seasonally, toppling guardians that cannot swim or climb.

# Human Families 01-05 — criminals / gangsters / thugs / rebels / mercenaries (25 entries, LOCKED, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free (revised versions). Taxonomy Human. All magic 1.

## 1. ENY_CUTPURSE — Cutpurse
Cutpurses work market crowds of lowland trade towns where coin moves faster than watchful eyes. They are opportunists raised in lean years after the empire’s fall, when a full purse mattered more than honest trade. Most carry a short knife less for killing than for cutting strings and frightening victims still. Ordinary folk fear the slow bleed of lost wages more than wounds, and merchants beat them bloody when caught. Town watches punish them with whipping and branding rather than hanging, leaving scarred veterans back on the same streets. They run in loose pairs, one to jostle and one to lift, scattering once steel is drawn. A cornered cutpurse fights wild and low, aiming to wound a leg and escape alleys. Travelers sew coin inside sleeves and never chase a thief alone at dusk.
Combat: 2/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: wiry hooded figure, patched cloak, palmed knife. States: sidle / lunging slash / doubled clutch / sprawled with coins. Variants: 2M youth/elder; 2F girl/shawl woman.

## 2. ENY_BURGLAR — Burglar
Burglars break timber houses and store-rooms of forest and highland towns where walls yield to a quiet pry-bar. They know shutters and patrol-change hours, lore passed cellmate to cellmate since garrisons thinned. They avoid faces, preferring empty rooms to drawn blades. Households dread morning discovery, for stolen tools take seasons to replace. Courts hang repeat house-breakers, so elders carry rope-burns from near arrests. In open fight they swing crowbar and hammer with workman strength but no drill, only when trapped. They surrender quickly when outnumbered. Valley farms keep geese and barred doors against them.
Combat: 3/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: dark-wool laborer, pry-bar, sack, soot-smudged. States: crouch / overhead swing / stagger / face-down on sack. Variants: 2M climber/breaker; 2F lockpick/carrier.

## 3. ENY_SMUGGLER — Smuggler
Smugglers move untaxed salt and iron along delta channels and marsh causeways where checkpoints miss every reed. They fattened after the fracture made every border a toll. Boatmen and carters first, knife-fighters second, they know fords soldiers forget. Common folk both use and resent them, glad for cheap goods, angry when cheap grain undercuts harvests. Patrols seize boats more than lives, leaving debts that drive them back to water. Cornered they fight back-to-back with hooks and cudgels, tougher than cutpurses from wet cargo. They rarely murder travelers, for bodies draw soldiers. Their names change each port, no manifest bearing truth.
Combat: 3/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: oilskin waterman, hook-pole, tar-stained. States: balanced / hooking pull / reeling slip / sunk kneeling. Variants: 2M boatsman/carter; 2F river girl/ferry widow.

## 4. ENY_COUNTERFEITER — Counterfeiter
Counterfeiters file and wash coins in back rooms of market towns, stretching scarce silver thinner with each successor mint. Their crime needs no blade until found, when penalty is the noose. Often failed smiths or clerks who learned weights in honest shops before imperial pay stopped. Merchants fear them worst, for bad coin poisons every later trade. Informers are paid well, so masters trust no apprentice long and move presses by night. In combat weakest of this kin, flailing hammer and tongs in panic. They keep hired muscle when rich, else bargain and beg before swinging. Villagers test coins with teeth because of them.
Combat: 2/1/5/2, always_physical.
Appearance: scorched-apron artisan, burnt fingers, nervous eyes. States: cringe guard / hammer-flail / face-cover / slumped over coins. Variants: 2M filer/master; 2F clerk/washer.

## 5. ENY_FENCE — Fence
Fences buy stolen goods in locked shops behind chandleries and pawn-stalls of trade and timber towns. They never lift purses yet make theft pay, so watches hate them most. Many began honest brokers when roads ran, then learned unasked questions earn silver. Common folk pawn honest goods beside stolen ones unknowing, ledgers kept ciphered. Courts fine them ruinously to seize hidden stock rather than hang. A fence fights like a cornered shopkeeper, cudgel in hand, shouting for the watch while breaking law. He pays blood-money on the spot over trading blows. Shop-boys with staves sometimes stand with him.
Combat: 3/1/4/3, always_physical.
Appearance: heavy-coat shopkeeper, rings, ledger pouch, cudgel. States: arms-spread / chop / rib-clutch / seated among wares. Variants: 2M youth/elder; 2F widow/clerk.

## 6. ENY_GANG_LOOKOUT — Gang Lookout
Lookouts are the eyes of street gangs in crowded market and dock quarters where alleys turn fast. Mostly youths paid in scraps, they whistle and scatter at the first helm. They know every rooftop gap and cellar door from childhood games turned to work. Folk ignore them as stray children until a robbery follows their signal. Watches rarely hang them, settling for beatings that harden them into full members. In a fight they are weakest of the gang, throwing stones and knives from distance before fleeing. Cornered they drop blades and beg, having seen what happens to those who resist. Their absence on a street warns travelers more than their presence.
Combat: 2/1/3/1, always_physical.
Appearance: ragged youth, sling and signal-whistle, barefoot quick. States: perched watch / stone-throw / cowering / bound seated. Variants: 2M street boy/roof runner; 2F market girl/alley sister.

## 7. ENY_GANG_ENFORCER — Gang Enforcer
Enforcers collect debts and hold corners for gang bosses who claim streets the successor guards cannot patrol. They are broad laborers and discharged soldiers turned to muscle after imperial pay dried. Shopkeepers fear their knock more than thieves, for they break fingers as ledgers. Courts jail them when caught but bosses pay fines quickly. In open combat they are the gang’s true fighters, swinging clubs and chains with brawler skill. They stand ground where cutpurses flee, guarding runners behind them. They spare those who pay promptly and maim those who stall as example. Travelers learn to walk wide around their claimed lanes at night.
Combat: 5/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: heavy brawler, club and chain, scarred brow, sleeveless vest. States: squared stance / club-sweep / head-shake stagger / flat on back. Variants: 2M young muscle/pockmarked veteran; 2F hard brawler/older matron.

## 8. ENY_LOAN_SHARK — Loan Shark
Loan sharks lend seed-grain and shop-stakes at crushing rates in towns where honest lenders fear to tread. Often failed merchants themselves, they keep neat books of ruinous debts. Debtors fear harvest more than winter, for the shark’s men come with the threshing. Law allows the paper but not the beatings, a line sharks cross nightly. They fight poorly in person, hiding behind enforcers, striking only with dagger when trapped. Their power is the ledger, not the blade, and burning it frees whole streets. Folk whisper their names but sign anyway when children hunger. A dead shark’s debts pass to crueler kin.
Combat: 2/1/5/4, always_physical.
Appearance: sleek moneylender, ink-stained cuffs, dagger cane, heavy purse. States: raised-palm order / cane-jab / clutching coat / kneeling over torn ledger. Variants: 2M slick clerk/grey master; 2F sharp widow/young reckoner.

## 9. ENY_PIT_BOSS — Pit Boss
Pit bosses run illegal fighting cellars in ore and garrison towns where bored laborers bet wages on blood. They were brawlers themselves before age taught them to profit from younger fists. Crowds love and fear them, for they fix bouts and punish talkers. Authorities raid the pits but bribed guards warn ahead. In combat bosses remain dangerous, fighting with knuckle-irons and grappler craft. They duel travelers who mock the house, seeking to shame rather than kill. Their champions stand beside them in real trouble. Villagers blame the pits for every missing son.
Combat: 4/1/3/4, always_physical.
Appearance: barrel-chested veteran, irons on fists, betting slate at hip. States: rolling shoulders / hook-uppercut / spitting stagger / sprawled across table. Variants: 2M scarred youth/fat elder; 2F hard fighter/older keeper.

## 10. ENY_SMUGGLER_BOSS — Contraband Boss
Contraband bosses command whole smuggling crews along deltas and marsh roads, above common smugglers in coin and cruelty. They own boats and carts bought with untaxed profit, paying river folk to look away. Unlike lone smugglers they bribe patrols and hire blades, growing bold enough to tax others. Folk call them river kings in jest, then pay the jest-toll. Law wants them more than any thief, but witnesses vanish. In battle they fight with sword and purse, flanked by crew, retreating by boat when pressed. They murder informers as policy, leaving bodies at fords. Their cipher-books map every hidden landing.
Combat: 4/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: fur-trimmed captain, short sword, boat-cloak, signet of debt. States: arms-folded command / sword-cut / reeling to rail / slumped over charts. Variants: 2M young captain/grey boss; 2F river queen/older quartermaster.

## 11. ENY_ROADSIDE_THUG — Roadside Thug (reuse)
Roadside thugs haunt thin stretches of trade road between farm belts and forest edges where patrols pass seldom. They are failed laborers and deserters who learned that a drawn knife earns faster than a hoe. Travelers fear their whistle from treelines more than wolves. Villages nearby pay them off with grain rather than blood. Law hangs them when caught but the roads are long. In combat straightforward and brutal, rushing with club and knife, no feint. They break at first organized resistance yet maul lone walkers. They share loot in rough equal parts and abandon wounded freely.
Combat: reuse ENY_ROADSIDE_THUG (3/1/2/2).
Appearance: ragged footpad, club, patched leathers, road-dust. States: roadside slouch / rushing chop / reeling / face-down in ditch. Variants: 2M young deserter/bearded ambusher; 2F hard marcher/older camp keeper.

## 12. ENY_TOLL_BRUTE — Toll Brute
Toll brutes seize bridges and fords, demanding coin from every cart that must cross. Bigger than cutpurses, they rely on the river doing half the threatening. Boatmen and carters hate them worst, for no detour exists. Petty lords sometimes license them, taking cuts. In battle they fight with staff and weight, shoving victims toward water. They hold ground at narrow crossings where numbers count less. They flee only when crossings are held against them. Their toll-marks scar posts for seasons.
Combat: 4/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: hulking guard, quarterstaff, toll-box at hip, wet boots. States: arms-crossed block / staff-shove / staggered spin / toppled into mud. Variants: 2M young heavy/bearded keeper; 2F broad warden/older collector.

## 13. ENY_ALLEY_MUGGER — Alley Mugger
Alley muggers work poor quarters of trade towns where lanes narrow past shuttered shops. Quicker than toll brutes and crueler than cutpurses, they strike suddenly from doorways. Night walkers fear their paired rush, with one holding victims while another strips valuables. They know watch-bells well and vanish before pursuing boots arrive. In combat, they swarm lone victims with knives, scattering from organized guards. They target drunks and strangers, sparing locals who might identify them later. Their cuts are shallow but numerous, leaving victims frightened and bleeding. Shuttered shops and narrow lanes give these attackers reliable cover.
Combat: 3/1/3/1, always_physical.
Appearance: lean lurker, twin knives, dark wraps, quick eyes. States: wall-pressed wait / paired slash / knocked sprawl / pinned seated. Variants: 2M wiry youth/scarred holder; 2F swift girl/older stripper.

## 14. ENY_CARAVAN_RAIDER — Caravan Raider
Caravan raiders strike slow wagons on open valley and foothill roads where dust hides numbers. Better armed than roadside thugs, they ride scavenged ponies and carry bows. Drovers dread their dust-cloud at passes. They ransom whole trains rather than murder, knowing dead merchants draw armies. In battle they harass with arrows then rush stragglers, avoiding pitched fights. They camp in broken country beyond easy pursuit. Their leaders keep count of ransoms like merchants. Strong escorts and horn-signals turn them aside.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: mounted raider, bow and saber, dust-cloak, stolen harness. States: mounted circle / diving saber-cut / unhorsed stagger / grounded with raised hands. Variants: 2M young rider/bearded chief; 2F hard rider/older wrangler.

## 15. ENY_DOOR_BOUNCER — Door Bouncer
Door bouncers guard gambling cellars and fences’ shops as hired fists at illicit doors. Chosen for size, they end trouble with throws rather than blades. Patrons respect their arms more than any law operating inside. They answer directly to pit bosses and gang enforcers. In combat, they grapple and hurl foes, remaining dangerous while unarmed. Weapons make them slower, though they can still block entrances. They hold doorways where tight footing limits an intruder's movement. They rarely chase trouble farther than the building's outer step.
Combat: 5/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: massive doorman, bare arms, grappling belt, bruised knuckles. States: doorframe block / grapple-toss / shaken head / seated against door. Variants: 2M young ox/older scarred; 2F tall wrestler/matron guard.

## 16. ENY_BREAD_RIOTER — Bread Rioter
Bread rioters rise in hungry towns when grain prices outrun wages after bad harvests. They are bakers’ wives, laborers, and youths, not soldiers, armed with stones and staves. Folk pity and fear them together, for their cause is just and their fire spreads. Authorities answer with water and batons first, steel only if stores burn. In combat they swarm and shove, dangerous in crowds, helpless alone. They break quickly once leaders fall. Their barricades of carts vanish by morning. Travelers avoid market squares on riot days.
Combat: 2/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: flour-dusted townsfolk, stave and stone, apron and shawl. States: shouting advance / stone-hurl / scattering stumble / seated with hands up. Variants: 2M young laborer/grey baker; 2F market wife/young daughter.

## 17. ENY_DESERTER_CAPTAIN — Deserter Captain
Deserter captains lead bands of runaway soldiers who refuse empty pay and endless marches. They keep drill and banners, living off forced levies in hill country. Villagers feed them from fear, remembering they were once protectors. Commanders hunt them as examples. In battle the most disciplined rebels, fighting in ordered ranks with spear and shield. They withdraw in good order rather than rout. They parley for pardons between raids. Their desertion spreads if pay stays thin.
Combat: 5/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: weathered officer, dented cuirass, spear, faded sash. States: ordered line / spear-thrust / shield-brace stagger / kneeling with grounded spear. Variants: 2M young lieutenant/grey captain; 2F hard sergeant/older quartermaster.

## 18. ENY_HEDGE_PREACHER — Hedge Preacher
Hedge preachers gather crowds in fields and barns, blaming tolls and hunger on corrupt stewards. They carry no weapon but their tongue, yet their words arm others. Listeners bring food and hide them from patrols. Authorities arrest them for sedition, making martyrs. In combat they fight weakly with staff, shielded by believers. Their strength is leadership, holding mobs together under blows. Capture them and crowds thin. Their sermons promise the old empire’s justice without naming heirs.
Combat: 2/2/5/5, cycle (hedge-sermon blessings flagged folk proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: travel-worn speaker, staff and satchel of tracts, hoarse voice. States: raised-arms address / staff-swing / bowed stagger / bound kneeling. Variants: 2M young speaker/bearded elder; 2F stern widow/young prophetess.

## 19. ENY_TOLL_BREAKER — Toll Breaker
Toll breakers smash toll-gates and burn tax posts along roads burdened with triple duties. They are carters and drovers who decided tribute costs more than rebellion. Traders secretly cheer while publicly condemning. Guards rebuild posts each time. In combat they wield sledge and axe against wood and men alike, strong from labor. They strike at dawn and melt into farms. They spare travelers and share seized coin. Their marks — broken beams — warn the next post.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: burly carter, sledgehammer, toll-token necklace of victories. States: gate-facing stance / beam-smash / shoulder-wrench stagger / sprawled on broken gate. Variants: 2M young driver/broad elder; 2F hard teamster/older smith.

## 20. ENY_STUDENT_AGITATOR — Student Agitator
Student agitators are clerk-school youths printing broadsides against stewards and leading marches. They are quick with words and pamphlets but slow with blades. Townsfolk admire their fire until angry crowds begin throwing stones. Masters expel them, while jails steadily fill with captured students. In combat, they are weak rebels waving knives and torches noisily. They rally others rather than personally winning difficult fights. Their ink-stained hands betray their work after every disrupted march. Dispersing the printing press causes the surrounding march to fade.
Combat: 2/1/4/3, always_physical.
Appearance: ink-fingered youth, satchel of sheets, short knife, school cloak. States: pamphlet-wave / knife-flourish / tripped sprawl / seated with torn sheets. Variants: 2M eager boy/spectacled leader; 2F bold girl/older printer.

## 21. ENY_FREE_SPEAR — Free Spear
Free spears sell their shields seasonally to employers feeding caravans and work gangs. They are landless farm children who learned drill from retired soldiers. Employers value their steady line more than exceptional personal courage. Villagers also hire them as reliable guards during harvests. In combat, they are solid spear-and-shield infantry, neither fierce nor brittle. They hold their ground while paid and leave when coin ends. They rarely hate their foes or pursue personal vendettas. Their contracts are sealed with handclasps instead of binding oaths.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: plain pikeman, spear and patched shield, marching boots. States: shield-wall stance / spear-poke / shield-bash stagger / grounded shield seated. Variants: 2M young recruit/scarred file-leader; 2F hard guardswoman/older camp guard.

## 22. ENY_COIN_BOW — Coin Bow
Coin bows are hired archers from forest-edge hamlets who track game and people. They guard herds and ambush rival convoys in exchange for pay. Their arrows feed employers before any larger battle begins. Folk hire them against wolves as readily as against thieves. In combat, they shoot from cover before withdrawing with knives. They avoid melee because their survival depends upon range. They quit lost causes early rather than waste valuable arrows. Their bowstrings are their livelihood, carefully oiled every night.
Combat: 3/1/4/2, always_physical (bows as physical).
Appearance: lean archer, short bow, quiver, green wraps. States: drawn-aim / volley-loose / knife-draw stagger / kneeling with empty quiver. Variants: 2M young hunter/grey marksman; 2F swift lass/older tracker.

## 23. ENY_CAMPFOLLOWER_BLADE — Camp Follower Blade
Camp blades are hangers-on turned fighters who cook, mend, and rob battlefields between hires. They know the leavings left behind by every passing army. Soldiers mock them but still use them for dirty work. In combat, they fight scrappily with cleavers and cudgels in packs. They loot first and fight only when loot requires it. Their carts follow every column searching for food and salvage. Peace leaves them hungry and frequently drives them into thievery. Their layered rags conceal tools useful for cooking, mending, and theft.
Combat: 3/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: grease-stained follower, cleaver, pack-cart, layered rags. States: cart-lean / cleaver-chop / load-drop stagger / sprawled on packs. Variants: 2M young cook/scarred scavenger; 2F hard cook/older sutler.

## 24. ENY_SIEGE_HAND — Siege Hand
Siege hands are laborers skilled with rams, ladders, and mattocks during walled assaults. Quarry and mine work has given them notably broad backs. Garrison towns dread their arrival even more than mounted knights. In combat, they strike slowly but crush foes with mauls and shields. They are deadly at gates but clumsy across open ground. They dig and hammer under fire when commanders require breaches. Their pay runs higher whenever a dangerous breach assignment awaits. Without walls to break, they hire themselves out as guards.
Combat: 5/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: massive laborer, maul, timber shield, rope harness. States: braced lift / maul-crush / reeling heave / seated on rubble. Variants: 2M young quarryman/bald veteran; 2F broad mason/older sapper.

## 25. ENY_WAR_DOG_HANDLER — War Dog Handler
War dog handlers lead mastiff packs for patrols and debt-taking where people are few. They raise pups using blood rewards and sharp whistle commands. Villagers fear the dogs more deeply than their masters. Local law holds the masters liable for every serious bite. In combat, they send dogs first, then strike behind them with spears. Handler and pack fight as a coordinated pair against opponents. If the dogs fall, their handlers quickly lose heart. Their leashes and whistles clearly mark them among ordinary travelers.
Combat: 4/1/4/3 (handler + dogs as one fighter).
Appearance: leashed mastiffs, spear, whistle-cord, hide apron. States: dogs-forward stance / spear-jab behind pack / dog-loss stagger / grounded with scattered leashes. Variants: 2M young kennel lad/scarred master; 2F hard huntress/older breeder.
