# Human Families 06-09 — hired swords / assassins / ninjas / militia (20 entries, LOCKED, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Assassins generic-only (no guilds). Ninjas Japanese role-names, no clans. Taxonomy Human. All magic 1.

## 26. ENY_HIRED_BLADE — Hired Blade (reuse)
Hired blades are practiced fighters whose discipline sets them above ordinary outlaws. They sell their swords to caravans, households, and threatened traveling parties. Daily drills keep their blades oiled and their footwork reliably sharp. Employers trust their steady hands when danger gathers around guarded gates. Villagers sometimes pool scarce coin to hire them against predatory gangs. In combat, they remain balanced and patient, parrying before measured ripostes. They withdraw when contracts end, rather than fleeing from sudden panic. Their reputation determines their wage, and broken contracts ruin future hires.
Combat: reuse ENY_HIRED_BLADE (5/1/5/4).
Appearance: clean sellsword, arming sword, buckler, maintained harness. States: balanced guard / measured cut / parry-shake stagger / kneeling with grounded sword. Variants: 2M young sword/weathered veteran; 2F duelist/older captain.

## 27. ENY_DUELING_TUTOR — Dueling Tutor
Dueling tutors teach blade manners to merchants' sons and guard officers. They take temporary hires between lessons to maintain their own standing. Their polished style comes from school-yards rather than harsh battlefields. Proud students boast loudly about the masters who taught them. In combat, tutors are precise and showy, preferring disarms over deaths. They may challenge travelers who publicly insult their favored pupils. Their costly lessons often earn more than ordinary hired work. A humiliating lost duel can empty an instructor's entire school.
Combat: 4/1/5/3, always_physical.
Appearance: slim instructor, foil and padded jack, chalked hands. States: salute stance / lunge-flourish / robe-tear stagger / seated with broken foil. Variants: 2M young graduate/older master; 2F sharp mistress/older matron.

## 28. ENY_BODYGUARD — Bodyguard
Bodyguards shadow wealthy travelers and paymasters, paid to bleed before clients. They are chosen for loyalty and vigilance rather than flashy swordplay. Their watchful eyes study crowds more carefully than individual blades. Households sleep more easily when a bodyguard stands beside doors. In combat, they interpose bodies and shields before striking clear space. They retreat alongside their charges rather than chasing defeated attackers. Every scar marks a life they successfully kept from harm. A dead charge can end a bodyguard's entire trade.
Combat: 5/1/3/4, always_physical.
Appearance: broad shield-bearer, tower shield, short sword, watchful scan. States: charge-cover stance / shield-bash / staggered shield-drop / kneeling over fallen charge. Variants: 2M young shield/older oathman; 2F stern guard/older protector.

## 29. ENY_ROAD_WARDEN — Road Warden
Road wardens are licensed blades patrolling trade stretches for toll cooperatives. They serve as part guard and part tax collector. Long patrols teach them every ambush bend along their assigned roads. Drovers gladly pay when familiar wardens display their protective pennants. In combat, they hold the road-center steadily with practical halberds. They pursue raiders with resolve but spare those who pay. Their recognizable license marks are copied and forged surprisingly often. Without wardens, thugs reclaim exposed roads within only several days.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: pennanted halberdier, road-cloak, license badge, horn. States: road-center plant / halberd-sweep / horn-drop stagger / seated at milepost. Variants: 2M young warden/grey patrol; 2F hard rider/older keeper.

## 30. ENY_RETIREDSERGEANT — Retired Sergeant
Retired sergeants sell drill and muscle after years of military service. They shape local militias and guard stored goods through dangerous seasons. Their seasoned shouts can still form disciplined lines from frightened rabble. Villagers pool funds to retain one throughout the difficult winter. In combat, they direct allies while striking short, hard, and cunningly. Their firm presence often anchors formations that would otherwise quickly rout. Their stories teach recruits more lasting lessons than their battered swords. Age slows their legs, but experience keeps their eyes sharp.
Combat: 4/1/5/5, always_physical.
Appearance: grey veteran, short blade, drill-stick, medal-stripped coat. States: stick-raised order / short chop / knee-buckle stagger / seated giving orders. Variants: 2M lame youth-trained/bald elder; 2F hard widow-sergeant/older drillmistress.

## 31. ENY_KNIFE_ASSASSIN — Knife Assassin (generic-only)
Knife assassins kill for coin where crowded quarters conceal a sudden blade. They accept single contracts, taking payment before and after each killing. They ask no reasons, only prices, timings, and useful descriptions. Folk fear their quiet approach more than soldiers' noisy patrols. The law hangs them without mercy, so they work alone and masked. In open combat, paired daggers make their first rushes especially deadly. They seek quick kills before escaping through the surrounding crowds. Their plain blades reveal neither masters, houses, nor easily traced origins.
Combat: 5/1/5/2, always_physical.
Appearance: plain-cloaked killer, paired daggers, masked wrap, soft boots. States: crowd-blend stalk / twin-stab rush / exposed stagger / crumpled with dropped masks. Variants: 2M young blade/pale veteran; 2F quiet girl/older widow.

## 32. ENY_GARROTE — Garrotter (generic-only)
Garrotters strangle chosen marks in dark lanes without spilling visible blood. They favor debt disputes and inheritance quarrels where silence pays best. Night walkers fear footsteps approaching from behind more than open threats. Garrotters carry wire in belt loops disguised as common tools. In combat, they grapple from behind and struggle face-to-face. If seized, they thrash, bite, and fight desperately for escape. They commonly work as pairs, with one holding and one pulling. Their distinctive wire marks condemn them when authorities catch them.
Combat: 4/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: wiry strangler, wire-loops at belt, heavy cuffs, hooded. States: trailing stalk / wire-pull grapple / thrown-off stagger / pinned with cut wire. Variants: 2M thin youth/thick elder; 2F small woman/older cleaner.

## 33. ENY_POISONER — Poisoner (generic-only)
Poisoners sell powders and draughts to clients who fear steel. They work from herb stalls and dye shops without drawing notice. Their knowledge distinguishes gut-wrenching herbs from valuable healing ones. Cooks and cup-bearers dread their hidden trade more than most. Proven poisoners lose their shops when the law takes action. In combat, they rely on prepared darts and rotating mundane dusts. Their advantage is readiness, which fades quickly after close engagement. They carry antidotes for themselves and seldom share them freely.
Combat: 3/1/5/2, cycle (trick-dust rotation, mundane).
Appearance: stained herbalist, bandolier of vials, cloth mask, careful gloves. States: vial-ready hover / dust-toss then stab / coughing stagger / slumped over spilled vials. Variants: 2M young mixer/pockmarked master; 2F sharp mixer/older dyer.

## 34. ENY_CROSSBOW_KILLER — Crossbow Killer (generic-only)
Crossbow killers shoot from upper windows and barn lofts at chosen marks. Many are failed soldiers or hunters preferring distance to personal daggers. Crowds scatter quickly when the first prepared bolt strikes nearby. They own no uniforms, relying instead upon rented rooms and cover. In combat, they fire once before drawing short swords when rushed. Their real craft is the prepared shot, never prolonged melee. They abandon crossbows readily when escape demands speed and mobility. Shuttered windows and zigzagging streets make their attacks much harder.
Combat: 4/1/4/2, always_physical (crossbow as physical).
Appearance: hooded marksman, folding crossbow, bolt-case, upper-window cloak. States: loft-aim / bolt-loose / rushed sword-draw stagger / toppled with empty case. Variants: 2M young hunter/grey shot; 2F keen lass/older widow.

## 35. ENY_BATHHOUSE_BLADE — Bathhouse Blade (generic-only)
Bathhouse blades strike where victims bathe or sleep unarmed and exposed. They pose as attendants, quietly learning habits over several patient days. Hosts fear them deeply because hospitality can become a concealed ambush. Their short blades remain hidden inside ordinary folded towels. In combat, they move quickly against unarmored foes with single daggers. Ready guards expose their frailty and quickly limit their opportunities. They flee through undressed crowds, using confusion as protective cover. Locked inner doors and personal guards effectively block their routines.
Combat: 4/1/5/1, always_physical.
Appearance: attendant-garbed killer, towel-fold dagger, damp hair, soft sandals. States: towel-offer stalk / close stab / seized-wrist stagger / wrapped in torn sheets defeated. Variants: 2M young attendant/older keeper; 2F maid/older matron.

## 36. ENY_SHINOBI — Shinobi (male-only exception: male role tradition, 4 male variants)
Shinobi are hired infiltrators who scale walls and watch roads for pay. They train upon cliffs and rooftops, valuing silence above brute strength. Households dread the faint scratches heard near shuttered windows at night. They carry hooks and short blades while avoiding open confrontations. In combat, they evade foes and stab from behind. They are weaker during sustained stands against prepared opponents. When pressed, they escape across roofs using practiced climbing routes. Dogs and loose gravel reliably expose their otherwise silent movements.
Combat: 4/1/5/2, always_physical.
Appearance: dark-wrapped male climber, hook-rope, short blade; lean, masked. States: wall-crouch / back-stab dart / tangled stagger / netted seated. Variants: 4M young creeper / scarred scaler / broad diver / grey watcher.

## 37. ENY_KUNOICHI — Kunoichi (female-only exception: female role tradition, 4 female variants)
Kunoichi pose as servants and errand women to overhear valuable secrets. They learn poison herbs and message codes alongside practiced smiles. Hosts most fear the familiar hands they once trusted completely. Hairpin daggers and powder gourds remain concealed within their clothing. In combat, they strike dazed foes after mundane ash flashes. Once exposed, they are frail against alert and ready opponents. They vanish into crowds of women when pursuit becomes dangerous. Their fans hide signals, while locked inner rooms block access.
Combat: 3/1/5/3, cycle (flash-then-strike, mundane).
Appearance: servant-garbed woman, fan, hairpin dagger, gourd at sash. States: serving hover / flash-slash / seized-wrist stagger / wrapped seated. Variants: 4F young maid / dancer / older matron / grey go-between.

## 38. ENY_GENIN — Genin (young low rank, all youthful visuals)
Genin are apprentice operatives sent on errands, watches, and first thefts. They are chosen young for their speed and small frames. They run messages through guarded streets using memorized lantern codes. Ordinary folk often mistake these youthful runners for beggars. In combat, they throw sparks and run rather than fight. Their quick legs are useful, but their blades remain unreliable. They memorize patrol routes, and sweets can purchase useful information. A caught genin often cries and reveals everything they know.
Combat: 2/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: youthful runner, ragged wraps, signal strips, quick feet; visibly teen. States: loitering errand / spark-toss flee / tripped sprawl / held with strips. Variants: 2M young boys / 2F young girls — all under twenty look.

## 39. ENY_CHUNIN — Chunin (older high rank, all mature visuals, higher stats)
Chunin lead cells and plan difficult entries after many experienced seasons. They read guards and weather, timing actions that others execute. Employers pay more for their judgment than their personal blades. In combat, they strike short and hard while directing genin. Their steady decisions anchor retreats that might otherwise become panicked flights. Their scars provide lessons that younger operatives cannot easily learn. Age may slow their legs, but never their watchful eyes. Losing a chunin can collapse an entire operative cell.
Combat: 5/1/5/4, always_physical.
Appearance: mature operative, quality wraps, command fan, grey at temples; visibly thirty-plus. States: fan-raised order / directed cut / winded stagger / seated giving orders. Variants: 2M older field-lead / grey planner; 2F older handler / grey coordinator — all mature look.

## 40. ENY_TEPPO_SHINOBI — Teppo Shinobi (matchlock ninja, flagged mundane firearm tech, no new magic)
Teppo shinobi shoot from lofts and field blinds using long matchlock tubes. This late craft was learned from foreign traders, without enchantment. Their single roaring shot can decide an ambush before blades meet. Crowds scatter quickly when smoke suddenly blooms around their positions. They rent upper rooms and abandon heavy tubes while fleeing. In combat, they rely upon one devastating shot before weak swordplay. Rain and damp weather ruin the powder they require. Shuttered windows and zigzagging approaches effectively foil their preparations.
Combat: 4/1/3/2, always_physical (matchlock as physical, flagged tech proposal).
Appearance: hooded marksman, long tube, powder-horn, rest-stick, smoke-stained sleeves. States: loft-aim / roar-loose / rushed sword stagger / toppled with empty horn. Variants: 2M young gunner / older shot; 2F keen gunner / older loader.

## 41. ENY_VILLAGE_MILITIA — Village Militia
Village militias are farmers and craftspeople mustered with spears and bells. They defend barns and homes when thieves threaten nearby families. They drill on rest days beneath retired soldiers' stern instruction. Neighbors trust these familiar defenders more than distant garrison troops. In combat, they bravely hold hedge lines despite raw experience. Heavy rushes can break them, though warning bells rally them quickly. Their strength comes from numbers and intimate knowledge of home ground. At dawn, they disperse again to their ordinary daily work.
Combat: 3/1/2/3, always_physical.
Appearance: homespun coat, spear and hide shield, harvest boots. States: bell-line stand / spear-poke / line-break stagger / seated with grounded spear. Variants: 2M young plowman/older headman; 2F hard wife/young daughter.

## 42. ENY_TOWN_WATCHMAN — Town Watchman
Watchmen walk night rounds carrying lanterns and halberds through market towns. They know familiar drunks and troublesome doors by name. Folk mock them during calm days, yet miss them afterward. In combat, they stand steady with halberds and call early. They hold narrow lanes rather than attempting open battlefield maneuvers. Their whistles summon heavier help when trouble exceeds their numbers. Poor pay leaves some watchmen vulnerable to tempting bribes. Honest watchmen nevertheless keep regular rounds through difficult weather.
Combat: 3/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: lantern-halberdier, town cloak, whistle-cord, badge-disk. States: lantern-swing patrol / halberd-sweep / whistle-drop stagger / seated at post. Variants: 2M young recruit/grey roundsman; 2F hard watcher/older keeper.

## 43. ENY_HEDGE_WARDEN — Hedge Warden
Hedge wardens guard field boundaries and orchards from poachers and boars. They work as solitary trackers carrying bows through tangled vegetation. Farmers feed them to keep watchful eyes facing outward. In combat, they snipe first before falling back with knives. They understand snares better than most formally trained soldiers do. Their dogs help track intruders and warn of approaching danger. Long winters thin their regular rounds across exposed boundaries. Briar scratches and muddy boots mark their persistent outdoor work.
Combat: 3/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: green-cloaked tracker, short bow, snare-wire, briar-scratched. States: hedge-crouch / snap-shot / knife-draw stagger / kneeling with empty quiver. Variants: 2M young tracker/older gamekeeper; 2F swift lass/older warden.

## 44. ENY_FIRE_BRIGADE — Fire Brigade
Fire brigades are bucket and hook crews who answer sudden fires. During riots or raids, they double as practical axe militia. Masons and roofers fill their ranks, making them strong on ladders. Settlements love them after fires but fear their strength during brawls. In combat, they swing axes and hooks with labor hardened force. Their experience balancing above streets lets them hold rooftops well. Brigade bells rally nearby militias whenever immediate reinforcements are needed. Dry seasons keep every crew alert, organized, and ready.
Combat: 4/1/2/3, always_physical.
Appearance: soot-stained crew, fire-axe, hook-pole, leather helm. States: ladder-brace / axe-chop / smoke-cough stagger / seated on buckets. Variants: 2M young roofer/broad mason; 2F hard carrier/older captain.

## 45. ENY_RIVER_PATROL — River Patrol
River patrols pole skiffs along channels checking toll tokens and smugglers. They know hidden reeds, shifting shallows, and reliable crossing places. Boatmen either respect their authority or attempt to bribe them. In combat, they hook and shove effectively from unstable boats. Ashore, their waterborne training leaves them notably weaker than opponents. They defend waterways, not broad land routes or fortified walls. Their narrow skiffs serve as mobile forts among familiar channels. Strong storms beach patrols and leave waterways temporarily unguarded.
Combat: 3/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: oilskin patroller, boat-hook, token-board, bare feet. States: skiff-pole stance / hook-pull / overboard stagger / beached seated. Variants: 2M young poler/older cox; 2F river lass/older inspector.

--- SOURCE: drafts/human_fam10-13.md ---

# Human Families 10-13 — soldiers / knights / paladins / royal guards (20 entries, LOCKED, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Soldiers lawful faction-neutral, no institutions. Knights no orders named (Squire replaced Lance Leader). Paladins fighters only, holy-magic never final. Royal guards generic-only, no courts. Taxonomy Human. All magic 1.

## 46. ENY_LINE_INFANTRY — Line Infantry
Line infantry march and hold with spear and shield for seasonal employers. They keep step where less trained militias bunch together. Villagers respect their solid line when it blocks narrow passes. In combat, they are steady wall-fighters, pushing forward and slow to rout. They advance together to the beat of military drums. Their strength comes from the shoulders of neighboring soldiers. Broken lines rarely reform once fear separates their members. Officers place them where a dependable wall matters most.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: uniform-coated spearman, shield, marching pack. States: line-advance / shield-push thrust / line-break stagger / kneeling with planted spear. Variants: 2M young levy/scarred fileman; 2F hard shieldwoman/older ranker.

## 47. ENY_CROSSBOWMAN — Crossbowman
Crossbowmen loose heavy bolts behind lines, often recruited from hunter stock. They need little training to kill effectively at distance. Defenders on walls fear the force of their volleys. In combat, they shoot then brace behind pavises with short swords. Alone, they are weak once enemies close into melee. They reload beneath cover while comrades hold the line. Wet strings slow their work and make each shot uncertain. Their heavy bolts can pierce mail at useful range.
Combat: 4/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: pavise-bearer, crossbow, bolt-case, padded jack. States: kneeling aim / volley-loose / pavise-drop stagger / seated spanning bow. Variants: 2M young loader/grey shot; 2F keen shot/older supplier.

## 48. ENY_SCOUT_RIDER — Scout Rider
Scout riders range ahead on hardy ponies, carrying sharp eyes and horns. They count enemy camps and burn fodder before larger columns arrive. Marching columns depend heavily upon their timely warnings. In combat, they skirmish with javelins and never stand fast. They flee dangerous encounters in order to deliver reports. Their hardy ponies outlast the heavier mounts of chargers. Hunger and lameness can ground even the most skilled rider. A warning horn lets distant allies prepare for discovered danger.
Combat: 3/1/4/3, always_physical.
Appearance: light horseman, javelins, horn, dust-cloak. States: far-circle trot / javelin-cast / unhorsed stagger / grounded holding reins. Variants: 2M young rider/wiry veteran; 2F swift rider/older guide.

## 49. ENY_SAPPER — Sapper
Sappers dig, bridge, and break obstacles using mattocks and mundane black-powder kegs. Many come from mining families accustomed to dangerous underground labor. Forts dread the tunnels and mines these workers prepare. In combat, they blast gates before fighting with picks. They are clumsy fighters when caught across open ground. They work beneath shields while others protect them from missiles. Their powder kegs are precious cargo guarded during every march. Floodwater can drown their mines before a planned breach succeeds.
Combat: 4/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: dirt-caked digger, pick, powder-keg, timber props. States: digging crouch / keg-blast then rush / deafened stagger / seated on rubble. Variants: 2M young digger/broad master; 2F hard miner/older powder-keeper.

## 50. ENY_STANDARD_BEARER — Standard Bearer
Standard bearers carry colors that hold fighting lines together through steady presence. They rarely strike, yet armies can break when they fall. New recruits watch their cloth for signs of ordered movement. In combat, they defend with short swords while lifting poles high. Nearby allies rally around the raised colors during confusion. Their loss spreads panic through troops who cannot see direction. Guards surround them whenever battle grows especially close and dangerous. Their cloth is carefully mended after every hard campaign.
Combat: 3/1/3/5, always_physical.
Appearance: tall bearer, pole-colors, short sword, braided cords. States: colors-high stand / pole-defense sweep / colors-dip stagger / kneeling clutching cloth. Variants: 2M young bearer/grey guardian; 2F tall bearer/older keeper.

## 51. ENY_HEDGE_KNIGHT — Hedge Knight
Hedge knights wander between hire and tourney with dented harness and one horse. They own no land, possessing only arms and their mount. Villagers hire them against dangerous brutes they cannot face alone. In combat, they are solid mounted fighters with lance and sword. They pride themselves on charges but become brittle in deep mud. They keep old vows of protection when an employer pays. Their horses eat before their riders accept any meal. A lame horse can quickly reduce a hedge knight to poverty.
Combat: 5/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: dented rider, lance and arming sword, patched barding. States: mounted salute / lance-charge / unhorsed stagger / kneeling with broken lance. Variants: 2M young errant/rusted veteran; 2F hard rider/older dame.

## 52. ENY_HOUSEHOLD_KNIGHT — Household Knight
Household knights guard manors and caravans for steady board and reliable pay. They are better fed than hedge kin who wander between employers. They drill courtyard youths in the basics of weapons and discipline. Households trust their gates and family members to these knights. In combat, they hold doors with disciplined sword and shield work. They retreat alongside their charges rather than leave them exposed. Their livery is plain cloth with no named heraldry. Their service remains personal, built on familiar daily responsibility.
Combat: 5/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: clean man-at-arms, sword-shield, household cloak blank. States: door-guard stance / shield-cut / helm-rattle stagger / kneeling shield-wall. Variants: 2M young sworn/older captain; 2F sworn swordswoman/older guardian.

## 53. ENY_SQUIRE — Squire (replaced Lance Leader)
Squires are youthful apprentice fighters tending horses and harness while learning arms. Chosen for promise, they polish equipment, carry supplies, and keep watch. Households feed them because they expect future capable blades. In combat, they are eager but half-trained with sword and buckler. They rush beyond their skill when a knight requires protection. They guard knights' backs fiercely despite their incomplete training. Their mistakes teach them faster than careful instruction ever could. Veterans eventually shape them into fighters or break their confidence.
Combat: 3/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: youthful attendant-fighter, borrowed sword, buckler, horse-brush at belt; visibly young. States: eager guard stance / rushing cut / knocked-down stagger / seated with dented buckler. Variants: 2M stable boy/page youth; 2F hard maid-at-arms/young page girl — all youthful look.

## 54. ENY_FOOT_KNIGHT — Foot Knight
Foot knights fight dismounted in harness with poleaxes at gates and breaches. Their heavy equipment makes long marches and marshes especially difficult. Forts prize them as defenders wherever walls face direct assault. In combat, they are crushing close fighters but slow to turn. They hold breaches against foes who attempt to force entry. Their harness needs squires for fitting, repair, and daily care. Mud can fell them before their weapons ever reach opponents. They prefer firm ground where their armor's weight becomes protection.
Combat: 6/1/2/3, always_physical.
Appearance: full-harness fighter, poleaxe, crested helm plain. States: braced gate-stand / axe-hook crush / toppled stagger / seated with raised visor. Variants: 2M young squire-raised/older iron; 2F hard fighter/older dame in harness.

## 55. ENY_TOURNEY_CHAMPION — Tourney Champion
Tourney champions live for prize bouts in fair towns and fame. Crowds chant for nameless favors when they unhorse celebrated rivals. They hire themselves as duelists during the intervals between fairs. In combat, they use showy precision with lance and sword. They seek dramatic falls rather than killing defeated opponents. They challenge travelers to contests at mounted passes along roads. Their prizes pay for armor, horses, and public display. A lost bout can empty purses and damage their reputation.
Combat: 5/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: bright-harness rider, ribboned lance, polished helm. States: circling salute / tilting charge / unhorsed roll stagger / seated accepting loss. Variants: 2M young gallant/vain veteran; 2F bold rider/older championess.

## 56. ENY_OATHSWORD — Oathsword (fighters + oath-blessings, holy flagged proposal)
Oathswords swear personal vows to protect travelers and sick people. They carry plain swords and ask no payment from temples. Folk offer bread for blessings these fighters cannot truly give. They walk roads where ordinary guards are few or absent. In combat, they are steady sword-and-shield fighters protecting the fallen. They never claim spells, relying only upon steadfast arms. Their oaths forbid flight when helpless people need protection. Broken oaths exile them from the trust of ordinary folk.
Combat: 5/3/4/4, cycle (oath-blessings flagged holy proposal per magic doctrine applicable-user; never final).
Appearance: plain-cloaked swearer, sword-shield, votive cord at wrist. States: vow-stance guard / shield-cut / kneeling prayer-brace stagger / prone over charge. Variants: 2M young swearer/grey keeper; 2F sworn sister/older matron.

## 57. ENY_CHAPEL_GUARD — Chapel Guard (fighters + warding prayers, holy flagged proposal)
Chapel guards watch small shrines and burial grounds without claiming priesthood. They sweep steps and turn away thieves seeking easy offerings. Mourners trust their quiet presence during difficult ceremonies and visits. In combat, they fight with mace and lantern-shield at gates. They hold thresholds rather than follow enemies onto battlefields. Their duty ends at the walls surrounding their charge. Their keys rattle whenever they make a careful nightly round. Thieves learn that quiet shrines may still have determined defenders.
Combat: 4/3/3/3, cycle (warding prayers flagged holy proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: grey-robed guard, mace, lantern-shield, key-ring. States: gate-stand / mace-swing / bell-drop stagger / kneeling at threshold. Variants: 2M young keeper/older warden; 2F stern keeper/older widow.

## 58. ENY_MENDICANT_PROTECTOR — Mendicant Protector (fighters + mendicant blessings, holy flagged proposal)
Mendicant protectors walk with begging healers, carrying staves against wolves and thieves. They own nothing except bowls, patched robes, and necessary walking tools. Villagers feed the pair when their charity can spare food. In combat, they whirl staves to shield unarmed companions without killing. They retreat alongside their charges when stronger enemies press forward. Their vows forbid loot taken from defeated foes or travelers. Their bare feet mark long journeys across rough roads. They depend upon mobility and goodwill instead of expensive armor.
Combat: 3/2/4/3, cycle (mendicant blessings flagged holy proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: barefoot staff-fighter, patched robe, bowl at back. States: staff-circle guard / sweeping trip / stumbled stagger / seated shielding charge. Variants: 2M young walker/older brother; 2F young sister/older mother.

## 59. ENY_RELIC_WARDEN — Relic Warden (fighters + relic-ward prayers, holy flagged proposal)
Relic wardens escort carts carrying old bones and banners between hamlets. They claim no power within relics, only a duty of care. Crowds follow the cart when it travels for seasonal festivals. In combat, they defend the wagon with halberds without advancing. Their loss scatters pilgrims who depended upon their visible protection. Their carts are plain wood, built for travel rather than display. Thieves repeatedly test whether the wardens can defend valuable cargo. Wardens keep close watch on every chest and wheel.
Combat: 4/4/3/4, cycle (relic-ward prayers flagged holy proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: cart-guard, halberd, relic-chest key, festival ribbons. States: cart-side stance / halberd-sweep / chest-drop stagger / seated over chest. Variants: 2M young escort/grey warden; 2F hard escort/older keeper.

## 60. ENY_PENITENT_BLADE — Penitent Blade (fighters + penitent zeal, holy flagged proposal)
Penitent blades are former criminals working through guilt by serving former victims. They wear ropes at their waists as visible signs of remorse. Folk distrust them first, then praise those who prove steadfast. In combat, they fight fiercely with flails and seek wounds as payment. They spare surrendering foes because mercy demonstrates changed intentions. Their past deeds continue to drive every difficult choice. Formal pardons release most from their chosen lifelong service. Some remain in service because forgiveness never quiets their guilt.
Combat: 5/3/2/2, cycle (penitent zeal flagged holy proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: rope-bound fighter, flail, scarred face, plain shirt. States: bowed advance / flail-scourge swing / self-struck stagger / kneeling with grounded flail. Variants: 2M young penitent/scarred elder; 2F hard penitent/older seeker.

## 61. ENY_PALACE_SENTRY — Palace Sentry (generic-only, no courts named)
Palace sentries stand at inner doors of great households, chosen for stillness. They memorize familiar faces, footsteps, and the rhythms of visitors. Residents ignore them until an alarm makes their vigilance necessary. In combat, they hold doorways with spears and shout before striking. They never pursue intruders beyond the posts assigned to them. Their shifts are long, demanding focus during quiet hours. Bribes test their loyalty almost every night of service. A sentry who recognizes danger quickly can protect an entire household.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: motionless spearman, plain household cloak unmarked, door-post stance. States: post-stand / spear-level / pushed-back stagger / kneeling at threshold. Variants: 2M young sentry/grey door-warden; 2F hard sentry/older keeper.

## 62. ENY_ESCORT_LANCER — Escort Lancer (generic-only)
Escort lancers ride beside carriages on open roads with bright lances. They drill in pairs to coordinate charges and protective screens. Travelers pay extra for the safety their visible pennants promise. In combat, they charge to scatter ambushes before screening retreats. They abandon baggage when necessary to save people under protection. Their horses are better fed than nearly every other mount. Lameness grounds a lancer and removes their chief advantage. A paired charge can open space for a carriage escape.
Combat: 5/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: mounted lancer, bright lance, carriage-shield, groomed horse. States: carriage-side trot / lance-level charge / unhorsed stagger / grounded shield-ring. Variants: 2M young rider/veteran; 2F hard rider/older leader.

## 63. ENY_VAULT_WARDEN — Vault Warden (generic-only)
Vault wardens guard strong-rooms and granaries of great households using paired keys. They count seals nightly and record any irregularity immediately. Stewards trust their tallies because stock losses threaten everyone nearby. In combat, they fight with short swords among tightly packed chests. They never leave the rooms placed under their direct protection. They may flood or burn stores rather than yield them. Their duplicate keys hang separately to prevent one easy theft. Torture threatens them when intruders seek access to protected stores.
Combat: 4/1/4/3, always_physical.
Appearance: key-hung guard, short sword, seal-board, iron-shod boots. States: chest-lean watch / close cut / key-drop stagger / kneeling over seals. Variants: 2M young keeper/grey master; 2F sharp keeper/older matron.

## 64. ENY_HONOR_BLADE — Honor Blade (generic-only)
Honor blades duel challengers who insult their households under strict counts. They practice daily to maintain exact timing and careful footwork. Their scars are displayed as evidence of past public contests. In combat, they are precise sword duelists seeking disarms before wounds. They refuse group brawls that make honorable counts impossible. Their seconds watch closely for broken rules or unfair interference. Lost duels bring shame upon the households they represent. They value measured skill over the chaos of battlefield fighting.
Combat: 5/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: slim duelist, plain sword, second’s sash, polished boots. States: salute / counted lunge / sash-tear stagger / kneeling yield. Variants: 2M young second/older master; 2F duelist/older mistress.

## 65. ENY_NIGHT_WATCH_CAPTAIN — Night Watch Captain (generic-only)
Night watch captains command household guards after dark, checking posts with lanterns. They know every corridor creak and each guard's expected position. Sleeping residents trust their careful rounds to reveal any threat. In combat, they direct guards with whistles and halberds on stairs. They rally sentries when an alarm breaks ordinary night routine. Their books log every entry, departure, and unexpected disturbance. Missed rounds can doom them when a breach follows unnoticed. Lantern light and disciplined patrols make their authority visible.
Combat: 4/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: lantern-captain, halberd, whistle, round-book. States: stair-stand / halberd-sweep order / book-drop stagger / kneeling directing. Variants: 2M young captain/grey commander; 2F hard captain/older mistress.

--- SOURCE: drafts/humanoid_fam01_gnomes.md ---

# Humanoid Family 01 — Gnomes (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 1. ENY_GNOME_SCRAPPER — Gnome Scrapper
Burrow bands of gnome scrappers scratch a living in scrub hills where thin soil defeats plows but hides warrens. They stand half a man’s height, broad-nosed and mud-caked, built low for tunnel work the empire once taxed. After the fracture left border farms unguarded, these bands traded winter labor for lifted grain and learned to fight farmers’ dogs. Ordinary folk laugh at their waddle until a whole pack boils from a ditch with picks, for numbers make them bold. They share food by strict pebble-count and exile thieves by burying them neck-deep till dawn, a justice travelers find crueler than whipping. In open battle they rush ankles with picks and hammers, brave in packs of six and cowardly alone, breaking the moment a shield-wall holds. They know every rabbit-run for miles and move camps each season, leaving swept burrows patrols find empty. Cold drives them down to storehouses, and only blocked burrows and fed dogs thin them.
Combat: might 3 / magic 1 / intelligence 4 / leadership 2, always_physical.
Appearance: half-height burrower with pick-hammer and mud-wraps, big-nosed and round-bellied beside a human child, knuckles calloused from digging. Small boots, pebble-pouch, tunnel-dirt ingrained. States: burrow-crouch ambush / ankle-rush pick-swing / hurled-back sprawl / piled surrender with raised hands. Variants: 2M young digger / grey elder; 2F hard mother / young lass.

## 2. ENY_GNOME_TINKER — Gnome Tinker
Tinker gnomes push whole workshops on their backs along farm lanes, mending kettles that successor smiths charge too dear to fix. Their people learned travelling trade when imperial roads still ran, and kept to it when tolls tripled, for a moving camp pays no hearth-tax. Villagers welcome the tune of their little hammers and count spoons after, loving the skill and fearing the lift. They trade sharpened knives for meals and sleep under barrows, telling tunnel-tales that borrow Frieren’s wandering warmth and Warhammer’s grumbling craft without claiming either world’s magic. Their oil-flasks and soot-gourds are mundane tools, and any “flash” is thrown ash, flagged never as final magic. When cornered they blind with soot then stab with awls, brave only behind smoke. Rain rusts their stock and ruins routes, forcing them to winter near mills. A mended plow buys them passage where spears would not.
Combat: 2/1/5/2, cycle (soot-then-stab, mundane).
Appearance: pack-laden mender with tool-bandolier and soot-goggles pushed high, smaller than a human youth, fingers black with polish. Barbed awl, spare handles, barrow-straps. States: pack-kneel offer / soot-toss awl-stab / pack-drop stagger / seated on tools with raised palms. Variants: 2M young apprentice / grey master; 2F quick lass / older dame.

## 3. ENY_GNOME_SLINGER — Gnome Slinger
Slinger bands hold scrub rocks above flock-runs, hired by shepherds to drop crows and thieves at a hundred paces. Their long slings descend from herder craft older than any successor levy, and their river-stone pouches never empty. Drovers pay in cheese to keep them posted, for a whistling stone warns before it kills. Like Tolkien’s hill-folk they love high places and hate to come down, fighting only from cover. When rushed they drop slings and flee down goat-paths humans cannot follow, useless in melee. Wind spoils their aim and wet straps snap, grounding them. Their challenge-whistles carry farther than horns. A broken sling ends a slinger’s season.
Combat: 3/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: rock-perched small figure with long sling and stone-pouch, scrub-cloak mottled grey-green, sharp eyes beside a human’s knee. Bare toes curled on stone, spare cords at wrist. States: rock-aim poise / stone-loose whirl / rock-fall tumble / grounded with empty pouch upturned. Variants: 2M young guard / grey eye; 2F keen lass / older keeper.

## 4. ENY_GNOME_BADGER_RIDER — Badger Rider
Badger riders are gnome outriders bonded for life to huge burrowing badgers, patrolling warren lands at dusk. The pairing began when pups were dug from flood-collapsed setts and hand-fed, growing into mounts strong enough to unhorse a man. Strangers laugh at the striped rush until claws open a horse’s belly. Rider and beast fight as one creature, charging low and clawing, and riders lose all heart if mounts fall. Their setts are fortress warrens no patrol will enter. Hunger makes them bold enough to raid storehouses. Meat tribute buys quiet roads. A fed sett stays hidden for seasons.
Combat: 4/1/3/3 (rider+beast as one fighter), always_physical.
Appearance: spear-carrying gnome astride a badger tall as a wolf, war-painted stripes matching the beast, bridle of braided roots. Rider’s legs barely reach the beast’s ribs. States: mounted patrol trot / badger-charge spear-drive / unseated sprawl / grounded clutching reins. Variants: 2M young rider / scarred master; 2F hard rider / older breeder.

## 5. ENY_GNOME_ELDER — Gnome Elder
Elders too old to dig keep tunnel-tales and settle burrow quarrels by counted stones. They remember droughts, soldiers, and the year the imperial tax-men stopped coming, and their memory is the band’s only archive. Bands obey their whistle-codes and share by their pebbles. In battle they stand behind young ranks shaking rattle-staffs, their weak thrusts mattering less than voices that hold lines. Their fall scatters packs into every ditch. They teach which farms feed and which set dogs, a lore travelers would pay for. Winter kills most, and a heard elder steadies a line better than a shield.
Combat: 2/1/5/5, always_physical.
Appearance: grey stooped gnome with tale-staff and counted-stone pouch, wrinkles deep as plow-furrows, wrapped in layered burrow-blankets. Smaller for age, eyes milky but loud. States: staff-raised order / short thrust / bowed stagger / seated giving orders. Variants: 2M old digger / blind singer; 2F old mother / tale-keeper.

--- SOURCE: drafts/humanoid_fam02_halflings.md ---

# Humanoid Family 02 — Halflings (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 6. ENY_HALFLING_POACHER — Halfling Poacher
Poacher bands live hedge-to-hedge along great farms they will never own, taking rabbits the game laws reserve for tables they never sit at. They stand half grown and move like field-mice, a craft learned from hungry childhoods after the empire’s granaries emptied. Farmers hang wire for them as for foxes, and catch them as rarely. Their short bows drop hares silently, and their snares feed whole bands through winter. When discovered they melt through streams, for water kills scent. In open fight they loose once then flee, useless against shields. Frost hardens their trade and sharpens hunger. A full woodpile and a blind eye buy more peace than patrols.
Combat: 3/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: hedge-small hunter with short bow and snare-wire coils, leaf-cloak sewn with pockets, bare feet black-soled. Child-sized beside a human. States: hedge-crouch wait / snap-shot release / dragged-up sprawl / bound with own snares. Variants: 2M young runner / grey eye; 2F swift lass / older keeper.

## 7. ENY_HALFLING_TINKER — Halfling Tinker
Tinker families push barrows of mended kettles between hamlets, singing for suppers. Their road-kinship predates the fracture, when imperial fairs paid for tunes. Villagers love the songs and count spoons after, a ritual both sides accept. They mend roofs and pots for nights in barns. When threatened they swing ladles and hot pokers in panic, brave only in crowds of kin. Soldiers scatter them with a shout. Rain swells their barrow-wheels and strands them. A mended roof earns them winter friends.
Combat: 2/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: barrow-pushing small figure with ladle-club and tune-pipe at lip, patched coat dragging near ground, flour on sleeves. Waist-high to a human. States: barrow-lean offer / ladle-swing panic / barrow-drop stumble / seated on wares with hands up. Variants: 2M young piper / older potter; 2F singing lass / older dame.

## 8. ENY_HALFLING_SLINGER — Halfling Slinger
Orchard slingers guard bustling fruit bands’ camps from piled stone forts built small. Their whistling hail can strip an entire crow flock mid-flight, a skill taught to children with green apples. Neighboring villagers hire them eagerly for fair-days and harvests. They love risky wagers and hate strict discipline. In battle they hail stones fiercely then scatter before closing shields, refusing melee. Their thin arms tire quickly under sustained throwing. Their river-smooth stones are gathered yearly from gravel bars. Strong wind foils them utterly every time.
Combat: 3/1/3/2, always_physical.
Appearance: straw-hatted small guard with sling and bulging stone-bag, orchard-dust on cheeks, perched on wall-tops at a human’s waist. Quick grin, quick feet. States: wall-aim poise / stone-hail whirl / rushed tumble / empty-bag kneel. Variants: 2M young guard / older captain; 2F keen lass / older matron.

## 9. ENY_HALFLING_DOG_RIDER — Dog Rider
Dog-rider scouts circle halfling camps on huge herding dogs, herding goats as readily as watching roads. The dogs are raised from pups alongside children, understanding whistles better than words. Strangers laugh at the charge until a ring of teeth closes. Rider and dog fight as one, dogs rushing while riders jab with short spears. Fallen dogs break riders faster than wounds. Their kennel-carts move with bands. Meat keeps loyalty; hunger makes them snappish. A fed pack stays silent all night.
Combat: 4/1/3/3 (rider+dog), always_physical.
Appearance: short-spear halfling astride a herding dog tall as a wolf, whistle-cord at neck, herding cloak flapping. Legs gripping shaggy ribs. States: circling trot / dog-rush jab / unseated sprawl / grounded clutching lead. Variants: 2M young rider / scarred master; 2F hard rider / older breeder.

## 10. ENY_HALFLING_ELDER — Halfling Elder
Elders too old to run keep road-tales and divide caravan shares by counting sticks. They remember famine years when bands ate seed-corn and nearly died. Their judgment decides which roads are safe. In battle they stand waving ladle-staffs, their weak swats less important than voices that stop routs. Their fall dissolves bands into quarreling families. They teach young which farms give and which set dogs. Age beds them in wagons. A heard elder holds a line.
Combat: 2/1/5/5, always_physical.
Appearance: grey shawled elder with tally-sticks and ladle-staff, laugh-lines deep, seated on wagon-steps at a human’s knee. Small hands busy. States: stick-raised order / weak swat / bowed stagger / seated telling. Variants: 2M old walker / blind singer; 2F old mother / tale-keeper.

# Humanoid Family 03 — Dwarves (5, exile bands only, no holds/clans as final, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free, every sketch sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 11. ENY_DWARF_EXILE — Dwarf Exile
Exiles are oath-cut wanderers camped in played-out quarries, forbidden the holds they still dream of. They stand broad and low, bearded, stone-eyed, carrying the shame-tallies they never show. After the empire’s pay stopped, many sold axes to whoever fed. Villagers hire them against brutes no militia can face. They keep exile codes strictly among themselves. In battle they are solid axe-and-shield fighters who hold ground and break only when surrounded. They respect anyone who stands. Work slowly buys parole-tallies.
Combat: 5/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: broad low fighter with hand-axe and scarred shield, quarry dust ingrained in beard, barely chest-high to a human but twice as wide. Heavy boots, tally-stick hidden. States: grounded stance / axe-chop / shield-rattle stagger / kneeling on shield. Variants: 2M young oath-cut / grey breaker; 2F hard exile / older smith.

## 12. ENY_DWARF_FORGE_HAND — Forge Hand
Forge hands tend cold furnaces in exile camps, mending arms for food. Their craft recalls grim forges without claiming runic magic, for their fires are coal and bellows only. Smiths respect their hands and test their welds. They remember hold-forges they will not name. In battle they crush with mauls, slow to turn, terrible at gates. They hold forge-doors to the death. Their aprons shed sparks. Quenched steel defines their pride.
Combat: 5/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: soot-aproned broad figure with forge-maul and burn-scarred forearms, beard braided back from fire, heat-shimmer around. States: forge-brace / maul-crush / burn-cough stagger / seated on anvil-block. Variants: 2M young stoker / broad master; 2F hard smith / older keeper.

## 13. ENY_DWARF_TUNNEL_GUARD — Tunnel Guard
Tunnel guards watch mine-mouths and cellar camps against thieves, masters of dark and timber props. They learned the trade holding real mines before exile. Miners sleep sound behind their lanterns. They hate open sky and fight best under stone. In tight tunnels their short-spear ranks are deadly; in open fields they are clumsy. They never abandon posts. Their lantern-codes mark bounds. Cave-ins haunt their dreams.
Combat: 4/1/4/3, always_physical.
Appearance: lantern-helmed low guard with short spear and prop-timber shield, eyes narrowed to dark, beard tucked. Shoulder-high to a human. States: mouth-stand vigil / tight thrust / lantern-drop stagger / kneeling at props. Variants: 2M young picket / grey warden; 2F stern guard / older keeper.

## 14. ENY_DWARF_ORE_THIEF — Ore Thief
Ore thieves are exiles who pry rich veins from guarded workings, despised by guards and exiles alike. They assay by tooth and smell. Fences ask no questions of their sacks. They know every fault and water-seep. In battle they skirmish with picks and flee real soldiers, fighting only cornered. Their heavy sacks betray them. Assayers cheat them routinely. A rich strike funds pardon dreams that never come.
Combat: 3/1/4/1, always_physical.
Appearance: narrow creeper with pick and bulging ore-sack, dark lamp hooded, fingers stained green-black. Low and quick. States: vein-crouch pry / pick-jab flee / sack-drop stagger / pinned on sack. Variants: 2M young creeper / wiry elder; 2F quick lass / older sorter.

## 15. ENY_DWARF_STONE_SINGER — Stone Singer
Singers too old to swing keep hammer-timing songs that synchronize whole crews. Their tunes map safe rhythms, never naming holds. Bands work faster under song. They settle pay-shares with verses. In battle they stand shaking drum-staffs, their weak taps less than voices that prevent routs. Their fall stills every hammer. They teach young the old cadences. Silence follows them.
Combat: 2/1/5/5, always_physical.
Appearance: grey singer with drum-staff and song-tallies knotted in beard, dust-white, seated on stone at a human’s knee. Hands trembling, voice strong. States: staff-raised beat / weak tap / bowed stagger / seated singing. Variants: 2M old singer / blind drummer; 2F old wife / tale-keeper.

# Humanoid Family 04 — Goblins (5, feral-only, no females, distinct from integrated settlement, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. All-male variants with reason (goblins have no females per WORLD_BIBLE). Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 16. ENY_FERAL_GOBLIN — Feral Goblin (reuse)
Feral bands haunt scrub and ditch country outside every settlement, wholly separate from any integrated townsfolk, implying no faction change. They stand child-high, green-skinned and sharp-eared, a people shaped by scarcity into spite. Since the empire’s granaries failed they have lived on lifted chickens and ditch roots. Travelers dread their night whistles more than wolves. They share loot by bite-order. In battle they rush with crude knives in shrieking packs, scattering the instant a shield-wall holds. They know every gully for miles. Their camps shift nightly.
Combat: reuse ENY_FERAL_GOBLIN (3/1/4/2).
Appearance: child-high raider with crude knife and rag wraps, mud-painted face, ears pricked. Light as a sack. States: ditch-crouch / pack-rush shriek / scattered tumble / piled surrender. Variants: 4M squeaker / cutter / sneak / boss-kin — no females by biology.

## 17. ENY_GOBLIN_WOLF_RIDER — Goblin Wolf Rider
Wolf riders are goblin scouts mounted on lean wild dogs reared on scraps. The pups imprint on riders and answer whistles. Drovers dread their circling yips at dusk. They scout camps and run down stragglers. In battle dogs rush while riders stab with short spears, the pair fighting as one. Fallen mounts rout riders instantly. Their dens stink of meat and fear. Tribute meat buys brief quiet.
Combat: 4/1/3/2 (rider+beast).
Appearance: small goblin astride a wild dog, short spear couched, rope bridle, trophy teeth strung. Rider’s feet dangle in fur. States: circling yip / dog-rush stab / unseated sprawl / grounded clutching lead. Variants: 4M pup rider / scarred leader / grey trainer / fat breeder.

## 18. ENY_GOBLIN_TRAPPER — Goblin Trapper
Trappers set wire and pit traps along footpaths, then sell “safe passage” they themselves threaten. They learn game-runs better than any warden. Farmers hang them on sight. Their pits take deer as readily as men. In battle they lure pursuers onto wires then stone from cover, helpless in open melee. Their own wires betray them as often as foes. Frost ruins their craft. A sprung pit warns whole parishes.
Combat: 3/1/5/1, always_physical.
Appearance: wire-laden small setter with pit-digger and stone-pouch, thorn-cloak, fingers scarred by wire. Crouching low. States: pit-crouch wait / wire-spring stone-hail / netted stagger / pinned by own wire. Variants: 4M young setter / grey layer / scarred bait / old toothless.

## 19. ENY_GOBLIN_SPEAKER — Goblin Speaker (no true magic, tricks only)
Ash-daubed speakers lead bands with chants and stink-pots, claiming spirit-voices. Their power is soot, noise, and nettle-smoke, flagged never as final magic. Bands fight harder under drums. They daub masks that terrify children. Travelers mistake the chant for war-preparation. In battle they hurl stink-pots then prod with staves, rallying young around the stench. Their fall scatters packs. Rain washes their paint and their authority together.
Combat: 2/1/5/4, cycle (stink-then-prod, mundane).
Appearance: ash-daubed small chanter with bark mask and stink-gourds, rattle-staff shaking, eyes bright through paint. States: chant-shake / pot-toss prod / mask-crack stagger / seated with broken rattle. Variants: 4M young painter / scarred chanter / grey hummer / toothless elder.

## 20. ENY_GOBLIN_BIG_BOSS — Goblin Big Boss (band-level only)
Big bosses are the largest goblins, holding bands by bites and meat-shares. They seize the best scraps and the driest sleeping holes. Young challengers test them nightly. Their junk-pile hoards are worthless to men. In battle they bludgeon with nail-clubs, standing while lesser goblins flee, seeking single foes to crush. Their death dissolves bands into squabbling packs. Rivals from other ditches absorb survivors. A fed band follows quietly.
Combat: 5/1/2/3, always_physical.
Appearance: oversized goblin with nail-club and scrap-metal crown, scarred bulk towering over kin yet still short to a human. Belly fat, arms long. States: chest-beat stand / club-sweep / belly-clutch stagger / sprawled on junk. Variants: 4M young challenger / scarred holder / grey tyrant / fat hoarder.

--- SOURCE: drafts/humanoid_fam05_hobgoblins.md ---

# Humanoid Family 05 — Hobgoblins (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 21. ENY_HOBGOBLIN_BRUTE — Hobgoblin Brute
Brutes are tall goblin-kin who sell disciplined muscle to any band that feeds. They stand near human height, orange-skinned and tusked, keeping crude drill learned aping old imperial manuals. Villagers fear their patrol lines more than goblin packs. They keep ranks where lesser kin mob. In battle they advance with halberds in lockstep, holding where goblins flee and withdrawing only on horn-calls. Their pay is meat and first sleep. Empty camps scatter them.
Combat: 5/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: tall orange fighter with halberd and rank-cloak, tusked jaw, scarred knuckles, nearly human-statured. Broad shoulders. States: rank-advance / halberd-sweep / rank-break stagger / kneeling with grounded pole. Variants: 2M young levy / scarred fileman; 2F hard fighter / older matron.

## 22. ENY_HOBGOBLIN_ARCHER — Hobgoblin Archer
Archer files loose disciplined volleys from scrub ridges, fletching their own shafts. Their craft recalls grim archer lines without claiming any chaos taint. Caravans dread their ranging whistles. They range every trail. In battle they shoot from cover then fall back with short swords, avoiding melee. Their strings are oiled nightly. Rain slows them badly. Their notched shafts mark kills.
Combat: 4/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: ridge archer with longbow and shaft-bundle, eye-scar, ridge-cloak, tall frame bent to aim. States: ridge-aim / volley-loose / sword-draw stagger / kneeling with empty bundle. Variants: 2M young bow / grey eye; 2F keen lass / older keeper.

## 23. ENY_HOBGOBLIN_SERGEANT — Hobgoblin Sergeant (band-level)
Sergeants drill mixed goblin packs with shouts and cudgels, turning mobs into files. They count heads and meat-shares on notched sticks. Bands march straighter and steal more under them. They rose from brutes by surviving. In battle they stand shouting orders while cracking cudgel-swords, anchoring lines that would otherwise melt. Their fall routs packs instantly. New sergeants rise by the same cudgel.
Combat: 4/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: thick-throated driver with cudgel-sword and tally-sticks, scarred voice-box, bulk looming over goblins. States: stick-raised order / cudgel-crack / throat-hit stagger / seated giving orders. Variants: 2M young driver / grey tyrant; 2F hard driver / older dam.

## 24. ENY_HOBGOBLIN_RAIDER — Hobgoblin Raider
Raiders ride scavenged ponies to outflank patrols and burn stores. They learn ground the way outriders do, circling wide. Outposts fear dawn dust. They scout by night and strike at first light. In battle they circle slashing with sabers, never standing to trade blows. Their ponies tire fast and limit range. Fodder dictates campaigns. A lame horse grounds the whole file.
Combat: 4/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: mounted tall raider with saber and dust-cloak, stolen saddle, pony shaggy. High in stirrups. States: circling trot / saber-slash / unhorsed sprawl / grounded holding reins. Variants: 2M young rider / bearded chief; 2F hard rider / older wrangler.

## 25. ENY_HOBGOBLIN_WARLORD — Hobgoblin Warlord (band-level only, no faction)
Warlords hold large bands by victories and meat-feasts, challenged nightly by young brutes. They wear trophy-cloaks and keep iron helms. Bands follow winners without question. They claim no territory or tribute-law, only the next raid, kept deliberately below faction scale. In battle they seek enemy champions with great swords, dueling while files clash around. Their death splits bands into rival packs that stronger neighbors absorb. A full belly keeps uneasy peace longer than oaths.
Combat: 6/1/3/4, always_physical.
Appearance: great-sword chief with trophy-cloak and iron helm, scarred bulk a head over kin, teeth filed. Imposing. States: challenge-stance / great-cut / helm-rattle stagger / kneeling with grounded sword. Variants: 2M young challenger / scarred holder; 2F hard chief / older matriarch.

# Humanoid Family 06 — Orcs (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 26. ENY_FERAL_ORC — Feral Orc (reuse)
Feral orcs roam broken country beyond the last fields, living by hunting and road-tribute. They stand broad and tusked, strength beyond any drill, shaped by the same hard hills that breed relentless war-bands without importing their gods. Since the empire’s levies dissolved, they have taken passes at will. Travelers fear their downhill charges. They respect strength alone and despise weakness in their own. In battle they rush with cleavers, breaking thin lines through sheer weight. They respect a foe who stands. Their camps are bone-piles.
Combat: reuse ENY_FERAL_ORC (6/1/2/3).
Appearance: broad tusker with cleaver and hide wraps, scarred bulk towering over humans, shoulders like boulders. Heavy tread. States: pawing charge / cleaver-sweep / shrugged stagger / sprawled on bones. Variants: 2M young charger / scarred breaker; 2F hard sow / older dam.

## 27. ENY_ORC_TRACKER — Orc Tracker
Trackers read trails for bands with noses better than hounds. They find the lost and the hidden for meat-shares. Villagers pay to mislead them with false trails. They learn ground the way rangers do. In battle they skirmish with javelins from scrub, avoiding stands. Their noses defeat disguises. Rain washes their craft away. A false trail wastes days.
Combat: 4/1/4/2, always_physical.
Appearance: lean tracker with javelins and nose-ring, trail-dust caked, nostrils flared. Loping gait. States: sniffing crouch / javelin-cast / blinded stagger / seated with empty hands. Variants: 2M young nose / grey sniffer; 2F keen lass / older finder.

## 28. ENY_ORC_MAULER — Orc Mauler
Maulers are pit-fighters who crush for sport and hire, breaking doors for gangs. Crowds bet on their bouts in hidden hollows. They were trackers or chargers who loved the crush too well. In battle they grapple and smash unarmed, deadly in clinches, slow to turn, helpless at range. They hold pit-centers. Their fists are maps of scars. Peace starves them into road work.
Combat: 6/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: massive brawler with bare scarred fists and iron collar, bulk straining wraps, jaw set. Towering. States: chest-beat / grapple-smash / shrugged stagger / seated on pit edge. Variants: 2M young pit / scarred champ; 2F hard pit / older matron.

## 29. ENY_ORC_SPEAKER — Orc Speaker (no true magic, tricks only)
Blood-painted speakers lead bands with drums and nettle-smoke, claiming strength-spirits. Their power is rhythm and pain-endurance, flagged never as final magic. Bands fight harder under the beat the way mobs surge to drums without any warp behind it. They wear skull masks. Travelers hear the drums for miles. In battle they drum then strike with staves, rallying young around the noise. Their fall quiets bands instantly. Rain stills their drums.
Combat: 3/1/4/4, cycle (drum-then-strike, mundane).
Appearance: paint-daubed drummer with skull mask and stave, smoke-gourds, massive arms. Voice like gravel. States: drum-beat / stave-crack / mask-break stagger / seated with split drum. Variants: 2M young beater / scarred chanter; 2F hard drummer / older mother.

## 30. ENY_ORC_CHIEF — Orc Chief (band-level only)
Chiefs hold big bands by single combat, eating first and sleeping warmest. Young chargers test them nightly. They claim no laws or lands beyond the next hunt, kept below faction scale. In battle they seek enemy champions with great axes, dueling while bands clash. Their death scatters followers to rival chiefs. Their rock-pile seats are thrones in all but name, never called such. A full hunt keeps peace longer than speeches.
Combat: 7/1/2/4, always_physical.
Appearance: great-axe chief with rock-furs and trophy-teeth necklaces, bulk dwarfing kin, eyes steady. Regal menace. States: axe-ground challenge / great-sweep / shoulder-wound stagger / kneeling with grounded axe. Variants: 2M young challenger / scarred holder; 2F hard chief / older dam.

# Humanoid Family 07 — Ogres (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 31. ENY_OGRE — Ogre (reuse)
Hulking ogres drift between bands as muscle for meat, too big for any single tribe to feed long. They stand twice a man’s height, slab-limbed and small-headed, shaped by the same hungry hills that raise brutes without importing their gods. Since imperial granaries failed they have lived on tribute extorted from farms too weak to refuse. Travelers spot their camp-fires first and walk wide. They understand bargains only in food. In battle they swing whole fence-posts, scattering lines through sheer mass, and shrug wounds that drop men. They chase what flees.
Combat: reuse ENY_OGRE (8/1/2/2).
Appearance: towering slab-fighter with fence-post club and hide belt, head small atop mountain shoulders, beside a human like a wall. Bare feet cracked. States: swaying advance / post-sweep crush / shrugged stagger / seated with dropped post. Variants: 2M young lumper / scarred breaker; 2F hard sow / older dam.

## 32. ENY_OGRE_CLUBBER — Ogre Clubber
Clubbers are young ogres hired as living battering rams by hobgoblin files. They love the crash. Quarry towns dread their arrival. They learn timing from sergeants’ shouts. In battle they charge gates and lines head-down, devastating on impact and helpless once turned. Their clubs are whole trees. Hunger makes them reckless. A held gate breaks them.
Combat: 7/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: young tower with whole-tree club and rope harness, eyes eager, shoulders raw. States: head-down paw / tree-swing crash / turned-flail stagger / sprawled on club. Variants: 2M eager youth / scarred ram; 2F hard lass / older matron.

## 33. ENY_OGRE_COOK — Ogre Cook
Cooks are rare ogres who learned fire-tending in army trains, boiling whole cauldrons for bands. They taste everything. Camps fight to keep them. Their stews stretch thin meat. In battle they defend cauldrons with ladles and cleavers the size of swords, furious when pots spill. They feed allies mid-fight with distracted generosity. Their fires mark camps for miles. Rain ruins their craft.
Combat: 6/1/3/3, always_physical.
Appearance: cauldron-tending giant with cleaver-ladle and tasting bowls, apron of sewn hides, nose running. Belly vast. States: pot-stir guard / ladle-crush / scalded stagger / seated over spilled pot. Variants: 2M young taster / fat master; 2F hard cook / older dam.

## 34. ENY_OGRE_THROWER — Ogre Thrower
Throwers are ogres with pitcher’s arms who hurl stones the weight of lambs. They learned skipping rocks across lakes. Outposts fear their range. They carry stone-piles. In battle they hail boulders from ridges then lumber down with fists, avoiding tight work. Their aim fades past fifty paces. Their piles betray positions. Broken fingers end them.
Combat: 7/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: long-armed thrower with stone-pile and sling-belt, one shoulder overbuilt, grin gap-toothed. States: ridge-windup / boulder-hail / finger-crack stagger / seated with empty pile. Variants: 2M young pitcher / scarred hurler; 2F hard thrower / older keeper.

## 35. ENY_OGRE_MATRIARCH — Ogre Matriarch (band-level only)
Matriarchs are eldest ogres holding mixed bands by food-wisdom and sheer bulk. They remember droughts and lead to water. Young brutes obey their ladles. They claim no rule beyond the next meal, kept below faction scale. In battle they wade in with iron-shod staves, shielding young behind bulk. Their fall scatters every hanger-on. Their cauldrons never empty while they live. Rivals wait for winter.
Combat: 7/1/3/4, always_physical.
Appearance: vast elder with iron-shod staff and cauldron-lid shield, grey hair in ropes, hands like hams. Mountainous calm. States: wading advance / staff-sweep shield / knee-buckle stagger / seated giving shares. Variants: 2M kin-guards — sons/brothers (reason: matriarch female-led line, males as guards); 2F old matriarch / daughter-heir.

--- SOURCE: drafts/humanoid_fam08_trolls.md ---

# Humanoid Family 08 — Trolls (5, outside-society bands, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 36. ENY_TROLL_BRIDGE — Bridge Troll
Bridge trolls are solitary hulks who claim fords and demand meat-tolls, wading where toll brutes merely threaten. They stand a head over ogres, moss-backed and river-stinking, kin to stone-folk without their speech. After the fracture left bridges unguarded they simply moved in. Drovers pay in goats. They count poorly and forget debts. In battle they sweep with whole pines, damming streams with bodies. They hate fire the way all night-folk do, though no magic burns them here.
Combat: 8/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: moss-backed giant with pine-trunk sweep and ford-mud caked, looming over humans like a boulder, eyes small. Dripping beard. States: ford-stand block / trunk-sweep flood / fire-shy stagger / seated in shallows. Variants: 2M young wader / grey mossback; 2F hard wader / older dam.

## 37. ENY_TROLL_HILL — Hill Troll
Hill trolls roam high pastures in pairs, lifting sheep and shepherds alike. They learn paths the way bears do. Flocks vanish whole. Shepherds fire the hillsides to drive them. In battle they hurl tors and grapple, slow but certain. They nap after kills. Their dens are thorn-choked. Winter hunger emboldens them.
Combat: 7/1/2/3, always_physical.
Appearance: pasture giant with tor-stones and shepherd’s-crook trophies, sun-cracked hide, loping gait. States: ridge-lurk / tor-hurl grapple / sun-dazzle stagger / sprawled on flock-remains. Variants: 2M young roamer / scarred taker; 2F hard ewe-lifter / older dam.

## 38. ENY_TROLL_CAVE — Cave Troll
Cave trolls haunt quarry deeps and cellar warrens, blind in daylight. They hear heartbeats through stone. Miners seal shafts against them. They eat anything warm. In battle they erupt from dark with claw and weight, terrible in tunnels, helpless blinking in sun. Their eyes are pale coins. Lanterns hold them. Their guano enriches fungi.
Combat: 8/1/1/2, always_physical.
Appearance: pale giant with clawed hands and quarry-dust, stooped under open sky, ears twitching. States: dark-eruption rush / claw-rake crush / sun-blind stagger / groping seated. Variants: 2M young digger / blind elder; 2F hard mother / older brood-keeper.

## 39. ENY_TROLL_RIVER — River Troll
River trolls lurk reed-beds to drag boats, cousins to bridge kin but swimmers. They learn currents. Barge crews dread still reaches. They trade with smugglers for rope. In battle they capsize then drown-grapple, weak ashore. Their reed-nests float. Nets tangle them. Drought strands them.
Combat: 7/1/2/2, always_physical.
Appearance: reed-crowned swimmer with hook-claws and boat-plank club, green-black, low in water. States: reed-float stalk / capsize-drag / beached flop stagger / netted gasping. Variants: 2M young diver / grey floater; 2F hard diver / older nest-keeper.

## 40. ENY_TROLL_HAG — Troll Hag (band-level only)
Hags are eldest troll females holding scattered kin by food-lore and threats. They brew bone-broths that stretch feasts. Young trolls bring tribute. They claim no rule beyond the marsh-edge. In battle they scold and swing iron pots, shielding young behind bulk. Their curses are noise, never magic. Their fall disperses kin for seasons. Their broths heal no wounds, only hunger.
Combat: 6/1/4/4, always_physical.
Appearance: vast elder female with iron pot and stirring oar, warts like knots, voice like landslides. States: pot-guard scold / oar-sweep / tripped stagger / seated stirring. Variants: 4F old hag / daughter / sister / grey aunt (female-led line, males absent with reason: sons eat separately).

--- SOURCE: drafts/humanoid_fam09_elves.md ---

# Humanoid Family 09 — Elves (5, flagged exile bands only, no settlements/culture as final, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free, every sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 41. ENY_ELF_THORN — Thorn Elf
Grey-clad exile bands haunt deep forest edges beyond the last settled fields. They move silently through undergrowth learned across centuries longer than human memory. After the empire’s foresters vanished, these bands kept old boundary paths without claiming any kingdom. Villagers trade venison for arrowheads while fearing their silent judgment. They share campfires sparingly and vanish before debts can form. In battle they loose long arrows from layered cover, retreating before shields close. They duel only when cornered, with slender knives and bitter precision. Hunger and iron shortage drive them toward farms they despise.
Combat: 4/3/5/3, cycle (elven arts flagged proposal per magic doctrine applicable-user; never final).
Appearance: tall slender archer wrapped in grey-green forest cloaks, longbow taller than a human child, eyes pale and watchful. Silver hair bound with thorn-cord, boots soft-soled. States: layered-cover aim stance / whistling volley release / flanked knife-draw stagger / kneeling with empty quiver. Variants: 2M young watcher / scarred ranger; 2F keen sister / older keeper.

## 42. ENY_ELF_DARK — Dark Elf
Cave-mouth exiles dwell in quarry shadows where sunlight thins and old workings breathe cold. Their skin holds ash-grey pallor from generations spent beneath stone without claiming any under-kingdom. They remember imperial mines they once worked for wages now forgotten. Miners trade mushroom-bread for their warning clicks about bad air. They hate bright noise and open battlefields with equal passion. In combat they strike from darkness with short spears, blinking helplessly under noon sun. They ransom captured lanterns for food rather than murder. Their clicking language warns kin across echoing shafts.
Combat: 4/3/4/2, cycle (dark elven arts flagged proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: ash-grey slender fighter with short spear and hooded dark cloak, pale eyes wide against lamplight, smaller shadow beside human bulk. Stone-dust ingrained, fingers long. States: darkness-crouch vigil / spear-thrust eruption / sun-blind shielding stagger / groping seated surrender. Variants: 2M young creeper / blind elder; 2F hard mother / older listener.

## 43. ENY_ELF_WOOD — Wood Elf
Antler-crowned wardens patrol ancient groves they tend without owning, marking boar runs. Their bands prune blighted limbs and burn diseased undergrowth each spring. Forest villagers leave salt at boundary stones for safe passage. They remember boundary oaths sworn to bailiffs long dead. They tolerate charcoal-burners while hating clear-cutters with cold fury. In battle they fight with long knives and snares among roots, vanishing between trunks. They never hold ground, preferring to bleed pursuers slowly. Their antler helms are shed finds, never forged crowns.
Combat: 4/3/5/3, cycle (wood elven arts flagged proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: tall warden wearing shed-antler helm over green-brown bark-cloth, long knife at hip, snare-wire coils across chest. Limbs scarred by briars. States: root-stance watch / snare-spring knife rush / entangled stagger / bound kneeling among roots. Variants: 2M young pruner / scarred warden; 2F keen tender / older grove-keeper.

## 44. ENY_ELF_NIGHTBLADE — Nightblade Elf
Moonlit duelists hire out as bodyguards to caravans crossing haunted forest roads. Their slender blades descend from dueling traditions older than successor courts. Merchants pay double for their night watches. They practice forms alone at dawn while camps still sleep. They despise noisy brawls and challenge hecklers to single combat. In battle they parry and riposte with exact footwork, frail against massed rushes. They withdraw when contracts end, never from fear. Their honor forbids looting the fallen.
Combat: 5/3/5/3, cycle (duelist elven arts flagged proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: moon-pale duelist with slender sword and parrying dagger, midnight-blue cloak clasped with leaf-silver, posture perfect beside human slouch. Hair braided tight. States: formal salute stance / riposte lunge flourish / overrun stagger / kneeling with broken blade. Variants: 2M young pupil / grey master; 2F sharp sister / older mistress.

## 45. ENY_ELF_WANDERER — Wanderer Elf
Road-worn wanderers walk alone between distant bands carrying messages and seeds. Their lifespan lets them remember roads the empire paved and successor tolls abandoned. They trade stories for shelter, never staying past three nights. Villagers call them luck when harvests follow. They avoid entanglements with courts or captains entirely. In battle they fight defensively with staff and short bow, escaping at first openings. They know every ford and famine path. Their seed-pouches replant burned groves quietly.
Combat: 3/3/5/3, cycle (wanderer elven arts flagged proposal per magic doctrine; never final).
Appearance: travel-stained walker with staff and short bow, seed-pouches heavy at belt, cloak patched across centuries. Eyes distant, boots resoled endlessly. States: road-stance readiness / staff-trip bow-shot retreat / pack-drop stagger / seated with raised staff surrender. Variants: 2M young farer / road-grey elder; 2F wandering sister / seed-keeping mother.

# Humanoid Family 10 — Treemen (2, intentionally low-count, generic for ents, Phase 1, no JSON)
Rule: city-free, every sentence 8+ words. Taxonomy Humanoid.

## 46. ENY_TREEMAN_OAK — Oak Treeman
Rooted giants wake slowly in ancient groves where oaks predate every boundary stone. Their bark hides eyes deep as knotholes, watching woodsmen across slow decades. They remember fires set by imperial charcoal crews and successor clear-cutters alike. Villagers leave damaged tools at grove edges as apology offerings. They move only when groves face axe or flame. In battle they crush with limb-arms, impervious to arrows, vulnerable to fire and axes. They pursue no farther than root-shade. Their fallen leaves bury the slain quietly.
Combat: 7/1/1/3, cycle (root-grasp behavior-magic flagged intuitive proposal per magic doctrine; never cast).
Appearance: towering oak-bodied giant with limb-arms thick as beams, knothole eyes glowing faintly, roots dragging like feet. Dwarfs humans threefold. States: rooted vigil stillness / limb-crush sweep / axe-bite shudder stagger / kneeling with split trunk. Variants: 2M old oak / scarred oak (male-presenting bark patterns, reason: slow seed-lines read male); fallback no females: bark has no sex, two age-forms.

## 47. ENY_TREEMAN_WILLOW — Willow Treeman
Slender willow giants sway over marsh pools where drowned roads vanish. Their hanging hair hides nesting birds and old nooses. They remember floods that drowned imperial causeways. Fisherfolk leave eel-traps untouched beneath them. They wake when waters are dammed or poisoned. In battle they lash with whistling branches, entangling boats and riders. They cannot leave wet ground. Their strands strangle slowly.
Combat: 6/1/2/3, cycle (lash-entangle behavior-magic flagged intuitive proposal per magic doctrine; never cast).
Appearance: tall swaying willow-form with curtain branches and pool-dark eyes, limbs supple as ropes, towering slender over humans. Algae draped. States: pool-side sway / branch-lash entangle / drained-wilt stagger / collapsed strands kneeling. Variants: 2M young withy / grey hanging elder (same fallback: sexless bark, age-forms).
